\displaystyle{\mathcal{J}}\circ i
\displaystyle=
\displaystyle-i\circ{\mathcal{J}},
\displaystyle{\mathcal{J}}\circ{\mathcal{H}}
\displaystyle=
\displaystyle-{\mathcal{H}}\circ{\mathcal{J}},
\displaystyle{\mathcal{J}}F(Z)
\displaystyle=
\displaystyle-F({\mathcal{J}}Z).
Thus if Z(0)\in X_{+} , the solution Z(\tau) of (1.21) will satisfies Z(\tau)\in X_{+} for all \tau in the lifespan.
Proof.
Those identities follows the definition of {\mathcal{J}} and i , {\mathcal{H}} , F(Z) . We only remark that \overline{\Delta_{b}}=\Delta_{-b} .
For the invariance, suppose Z(0)\in X_{+} generates a solution Z(\tau) . Since {\mathcal{J}}Z satisfies the same equation with initial data {\mathcal{J}}(Z)(0)=Z(0)\in X_{+} , the uniqueness of solution from Proposition D.2 indicates Z(\tau)={\mathcal{J}}Z(\tau) for all \tau , which implies Z(\tau)\in X_{+} .
∎
Define the exponent
\alpha_{c}:=d\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p+1}\right)=\frac{d}{d+2-2s_{c}}\in(s_{c},1)\cap\left(\frac{d}{d+2},\frac{d}{2}\right)
(6.5)
such that \dot{H}^{\alpha_{c}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow L^{p+1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}) .
Recall the local-wellposedness of (NLS) and (1.21) in \dot{H}^{\sigma} , \sigma\in(s_{c},\alpha_{c}) from Proposition D.1 and Proposition D.2 respectively. The identification of these two systems via {\mathcal{J}} -invariance follows.
Lemma 6.2.
Let d\geq 1 , 0<s_{c}<\min\left\{\frac{d}{2},1\right\} , \sigma\in(s_{c},\alpha_{c}) . Let u solves (NLS) with initial data u_{0}\in\dot{H}^{\sigma} , and Z solves (1.21) with initial data Z_{0}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\varepsilon_{0}\\
{\overline{\varepsilon}_{0}}\end{array}\right)\in(\dot{H}^{\sigma})^{2} .
Take any T>0 and \lambda(t) , \tau(t) from (1.3).
If the initial data are related by
u_{0}=\lambda(0)^{-\frac{2}{p-1}}(Q_{b}+\varepsilon_{0})(x/\lambda(0))e^{i\tau(0)},
then these two solutions identify by Z(\tau,y)=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\varepsilon(\tau,y)\\
{\overline{\varepsilon}}(\tau,y)\end{array}\right) and
u(t,x)=\lambda(t)^{-\frac{2}{p-1}}(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)\left(t,\frac{x}{\lambda(t)}\right)e^{i\left(\tau(t)\right)}.
(6.6)
Proof.
The form Z=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\varepsilon\\
{\overline{\varepsilon}}\end{array}\right)\in X_{+} follows the previous lemma. Using the equation of Q_{b} (1.5), we can check the u defined in (6.6) satisfies (NLS) with initial data u_{0} . Thus the local well-posedness Proposition D.1 implies the correspondence.
∎
Next, we show the spectral projection of {\mathcal{H}} from Section 4 also preserves X_{+} , so that we can decouple the evolution of (1.21) in X_{+} into stable, unstable and center parts.
Lemma 6.3 (Spectrum and {\mathcal{J}} -invariance of {\mathcal{H}} ).
Let d\geq 1 , 0<s_{c}<\min\{\frac{d}{2},1\} and assume the existence of Q_{b} satisfying (1.5), (1.6) and (1.7). For the operator {\mathcal{H}} in (1.4) with potential (1.26), there exists 0<\delta_{1}<\delta_{0} with \delta_{0} from (6.2), such that for \sigma\in(s_{c},s_{c}+\delta_{1}) , {\mathcal{H}}\big|_{\dot{H}^{\sigma}} has the spectrum decomposition
\sigma_{\rm ess}({\mathcal{H}}\big|_{\dot{H}^{\sigma}})=\{\mathbb{R}+ib(\sigma-s_{c})\},\quad\sigma_{\rm disc}({\mathcal{H}}\big|_{\dot{H}^{\sigma}})={\mathcal{E}}_{s}\cup{\mathcal{E}}_{u}
(6.7)
with the finite discrete sets {\mathcal{E}}_{s} , {\mathcal{E}}_{u} satisfying
{\mathcal{E}}_{s}\subset\{z\in\mathbb{C}:\Im z\geq b\delta_{1}\},\quad{\mathcal{E}}_{u}\subset\{z\in\mathbb{C}:\Im z\leq 0\}.
(6.8)
The corresponding spectral projections as P_{c}:=P_{\rm ess} 88
8
We replace subscription \rm{ess} by c for simplicity., P_{s} and P_{u} will satisfy
(1)
I=P_{\rm c}+P_{u}+P_{s} .
(2)
P_{s},P_{u}\in\mathcal{L}((\dot{W}^{\sigma,p_{1}}+\dot{W}^{\sigma,p_{0}^{\prime}})^{2}\to(\dot{W}^{\sigma,p_{0}}\cap\dot{W}^{\sigma,p_{1}^{\prime}})^{2}) for p_{0} as (6.2) and some p_{1}>2 .
(3)
{\mathcal{J}} -invariance:
{\mathcal{J}}\circ P_{\alpha}=P_{\alpha}\circ{\mathcal{J}},\quad\alpha\in\{s,u,c\}.
(6.9)
Proof.
The spectral decomposition is a direct conclusion of Proposition 4.5 with the \delta_{1} and decoupling (6.8) coming from Lemma (4.3). The only statement left is the {\mathcal{J}} -invariance property.
It suffices to prove (6.9) for \alpha\in P_{s} . From the boundedness and gap of {\mathcal{E}}_{s} and {\mathcal{E}}_{u} , we can pick contours \gamma_{s},\gamma_{u}\subset\mathbb{C} to be symmetric about i\mathbb{R} such that
P_{\alpha}=-\frac{1}{2\pi i}\oint_{\gamma_{\alpha}}({\mathcal{H}}-z)^{-1}dz,\quad\alpha\in\{u,s\}.
(6.10)
Thus \gamma_{\alpha}=-{\overline{\gamma}}_{\alpha} as sets but with reversed orientation as oriented curves. Denote J:=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&1\\
1&0\end{array}\right) , then {\mathcal{J}}Z=\overline{JZ} . Direct computation implies J{\mathcal{H}}J=-\overline{{\mathcal{H}}} . Thus
\displaystyle{\mathcal{J}}P_{\alpha}Z
\displaystyle=
\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi i}\oint_{\gamma_{\alpha}}\overline{J({\mathcal{H}}-z)^{-1}J}d{\overline{z}}\overline{JZ}=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\oint_{\gamma_{\alpha}}(-{\mathcal{H}}-{\overline{z}})^{-1}d{\overline{z}}\overline{JZ}
\displaystyle=
\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi i}\oint_{-{\overline{\gamma}}_{\alpha}}({\mathcal{H}}-\omega)^{-1}d\omega\overline{JZ}=-\frac{1}{2\pi i}\oint_{\gamma_{\alpha}}({\mathcal{H}}-\omega)^{-1}d\omega\overline{JZ}=P_{\alpha}{\mathcal{J}}Z
for \alpha\in\{u,s\} .
∎
Spectrally decoupled system (6.12) in X_{+} .
Applying these projections onto (1.21), we get the decoupled system
\left|\begin{array}[]{l}i\partial_{\tau}Z_{c}+{\mathcal{H}}Z_{c}=F_{c}\\
i\partial_{\tau}Z_{s}+{\mathcal{H}}Z_{s}=F_{s}\\
i\partial_{\tau}Z_{u}+{\mathcal{H}}Z_{u}=F_{u}\end{array}\right.
(6.11)
with Z_{\alpha}=P_{\alpha}Z , F_{\alpha}=P_{\alpha}F for \alpha\in\{u,s,c\} .
We further parametrize the evolution in X_{s}:=P_{s}X_{+} and X_{u}:=P_{u}X_{+} as \mathbb{R}^{d_{s}} and \mathbb{R}^{d_{u}} respectively99
9
We cannot view X_{u} or X_{s} as complex-valued space since X_{+} is only \mathbb{R} -linear..
Then the system (6.11) finally turns into
\left|\begin{array}[]{l}i\partial_{\tau}Z_{c}+{\mathcal{H}}Z_{c}=F_{c}\\
\frac{d}{d\tau}z_{s}+A_{s}z_{s}=f_{s},\\
\frac{d}{d\tau}z_{u}+A_{u}z_{u}=f_{u}.\end{array}\right.
(6.12)
where
\displaystyle z_{\alpha}(\tau)=\phi_{\alpha}(Z_{\alpha}(\tau))\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\alpha}},
\displaystyle\qquad f_{\alpha}(\tau)=\phi_{\alpha}(-iF_{\alpha}(\tau))\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\alpha}},
\displaystyle A_{\alpha}=\phi_{\alpha}^{-1}\circ
\displaystyle(-i{\mathcal{H}}P_{\alpha})\circ\phi_{\alpha}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\alpha}\times d_{\alpha}},
with \phi_{\alpha}:P_{\alpha}X_{+}\to\mathbb{R}^{d_{\alpha}} a \mathbb{R} -linear homeomorphism for \alpha\in\{u,s\} . Moreover, they satisfy
\displaystyle\Re\left(\mathrm{eig}(A_{s})\right)\geq b\delta_{1},
\displaystyle\Re\left(\mathrm{eig}(A_{u})\right)\leq 0,
(6.13)
\displaystyle|f_{\alpha}|\sim\|F_{\alpha}\|_{\dot{H}^{\sigma}}\lesssim\|F\|_{\dot{H}^{\sigma}+\dot{W}^{\sigma,p_{0}^{\prime}}},
\displaystyle|z_{\alpha}|\sim\|Z_{\alpha}\|_{\dot{W}^{\sigma,q}}\,\,\forall q\in[2,p_{0}]
(6.14)
from Lemma 6.3.
6.2. Finite-codimensional stability in \dot{H}^{\sigma}
In this subsection, we prove a more accurate version of Theorem 1.1. We first recall X_{+} space from (6.3) and define the natural isometry
\Pi:X_{+}\to\dot{H}^{\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\qquad\Pi\left(f,{\overline{f}}\right)^{T}=f.
(6.15)
Theorem 6.4 ( \dot{H}^{\sigma} finite-codimensional asymptotic stability).
Let d\geq 1 , s_{c}\in(0,1) , and b,Q_{b} satisfying (1.5), (1.6), (1.7).
Then for every 0<\sigma-s_{c}\ll 1 , the following statements hold true.
(1)
Finite codimensional stability: There exists \epsilon_{0}\ll 1 and a Lipschitz map
\Phi:B_{\epsilon_{0}}^{X_{+}}\cap P_{cs}X_{+}\to P_{u}X_{+}
such that for all \varepsilon_{cs}\in B_{\epsilon_{0}}^{\dot{H}^{\sigma}}\cap\Pi P_{cs}X_{+} , the initial data
u_{0}=Q_{b}+\varepsilon_{cs}+\Pi\Phi(\Pi^{-1}\varepsilon_{cs})
generates a solution of (NLS) blowing up at T=\frac{1}{2b} satisfying
\displaystyle u(t,x)
\displaystyle=
\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda(t)^{\frac{2}{p-1}}}(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)\left(t,\frac{x}{\lambda(t)}\right)e^{i\tau(t)},
(6.16)
\displaystyle\|\varepsilon(t)\|_{\dot{H}^{\sigma}_{x}}
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle(1-2bt)^{\frac{\sigma-s_{c}}{2}},
(6.17)
where (\lambda(t),\gamma(t),\tau(t)) are given by (1.3), and there exists u_{*}\in\dot{H}^{\sigma} such that
u(t)-\frac{1}{\lambda(t)^{\frac{2}{p-1}}}Q_{b}\left(\frac{x}{\lambda(t)}\right)e^{i\tau(t)}\to u_{*}\quad\mathrm{in}\,\,\dot{H}^{\sigma}\quad as\quad t\to T.
(6.18)
Here P_{u} and P_{cs}:=P_{c}+P_{s} are from Lemma 6.3.
(2)
Contraction of the unstable-data map: \Phi satisfies \Phi(0)=0 and
\|\Phi(\varepsilon_{1})-\Phi(\varepsilon_{2})\|_{X_{+}}\lesssim\left(\|\varepsilon_{1}\|_{X_{+}}+\|\varepsilon_{2}\|_{X_{+}}\right)^{\min\{p-1,1\}}\|\varepsilon_{1}-\varepsilon_{2}\|_{X_{+}}.
(6.19)
The statement (1) clearly implies Theorem 1.1.
Proof of Theorem 6.4.
We choose \sigma\in(s_{c},s_{c}+\frac{1}{2}\delta_{1}) with \delta_{1} from Lemma 6.3, and we denote
\mu=b(\sigma-s_{c})\leq\frac{1}{2}\delta_{1}\leq\frac{1}{2}\delta_{0},
(6.20)
where \delta_{0} is from (6.2).
We introduce the following exponents:
\frac{1}{r_{1}}=\frac{\sigma}{d}+\frac{1}{p+1}\frac{d-2\sigma}{d},\quad\frac{1}{r_{2}}=\frac{p}{p+1}\frac{d-2\sigma}{d},\quad\frac{1}{q_{1}}=\frac{(p-1)(d-2\sigma)}{4(p+1)}.
(6.21)
Using \alpha_{c} from (6.5), we have \frac{1}{r_{1}}=\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\alpha_{c}}{d}\frac{d-2\sigma}{d}\in\left(\frac{1}{2^{*}},\frac{1}{2}\right) , \frac{1}{q_{1}}=\frac{\alpha_{c}(d-2\sigma)}{2d} .
One can then check \frac{2}{q_{1}}+\frac{d}{r_{1}}=\frac{d}{2} , \frac{1}{r_{1}^{\prime}}=\frac{1}{r_{1}}+\frac{p-1}{pr_{2}} , \dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}^{\prime}}\hookrightarrow L^{r_{2}} and \dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}}\hookrightarrow L^{pr_{2}} . Moreover, recalling \delta_{0},p_{0} from (6.2), we have r_{1}<p_{0} since
\frac{1}{r_{1}}-\frac{1}{p_{0}}=\min\left\{\frac{d(1-\alpha_{c})+2\sigma\alpha_{c}}{d^{2}},\frac{d-2\sigma}{d(p+1)}\right\}-\frac{\delta_{0}s_{c}}{d^{2}(p+1)}>0.
We also define
\beta:=\frac{1}{q_{1}^{\prime}}-\frac{p}{q_{1}}=\frac{\sigma-s_{c}}{\frac{d}{2}-s_{c}}>0,\quad\tilde{p}=\min\left\{\frac{p+1}{2},\frac{3}{2}\right\}.
(6.22)
Step 1. Reduction to contraction mapping estimates. Let \epsilon_{0}\ll 1 . For any
(Z_{c0},z_{s0})\in P_{c}X_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{s}},\quad\max\{\|Z_{c0}\|_{(\dot{H}^{\sigma})^{2}},|z_{s0}|\}=:\epsilon\leq\epsilon_{0},
We denote \bm{Z}=(Z_{c},z_{s},z_{u}) , and define the linear map \bm{\Psi}(\bm{Z})=(\bm{\Psi}_{c}(\bm{Z}),\bm{\Psi}_{s}(\bm{Z}),\bm{\Psi}_{u}(\bm{Z})) as
\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\bm{\Psi}_{c}(\bm{Z})\\
\bm{\Psi}_{s}(\bm{Z})\\
\bm{\Psi}_{u}(\bm{Z})\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}e^{it{\mathcal{H}}}Z_{c0}-i\int_{0}^{t}e^{i(t-\tau){\mathcal{H}}}P_{c}Fd\tau\\
e^{-tA_{s}}z_{s0}+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-(t-\tau)A_{s}}f_{s}d\tau\\
-\int_{t}^{\infty}e^{(\tau-t)A_{u}}f_{u}d\tau\end{array}\right)
(6.23)
Define the Banach space as
\displaystyle\mathbb{X}=\mathbb{X}_{c}\times\mathbb{X}_{s}\times\mathbb{X}_{u}:=S\times C^{0}_{e^{\frac{\tilde{p}+1}{2}\mu t}}\left([0,\infty),\mathbb{R}^{d_{s}}\right)\times C^{0}_{e^{\frac{\tilde{p}+1}{2}\mu t}}\left([0,\infty),\mathbb{R}^{d_{u}}\right)
where the Strichartz norm is
S=C^{0}_{e^{\mu t}}([0,\infty),(\dot{H}_{y}^{\sigma})^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))\cap\left(L^{2}_{e^{\mu t}}\cap L^{q_{1}}_{e^{\mu t}}\right)([0,\infty),(\dot{W}_{y}^{\sigma,r_{1}})^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})).
(6.24)
We claim that for \bm{Z},\tilde{\bm{Z}}\in B^{\mathbb{X}}_{C_{0}\epsilon} with \epsilon_{0}\ll 1 , C_{0}\gg 1 , we have
\displaystyle\|e^{\tilde{p}\mu\tau}F(Z)\|_{L^{q_{1}^{\prime}}_{\tau}\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}^{\prime}}_{y}}
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle\|\bm{Z}\|_{\mathbb{X}}^{\min\{p,2\}}
(6.25)
\displaystyle\|\bm{\Psi}_{c}(\bm{Z})\|_{\mathbb{X}_{c}}
\displaystyle\leq
\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}C_{0}\left(\epsilon+\|\bm{Z}\|_{\mathbb{X}}^{\min\{p,2\}}\right),
(6.26)
\displaystyle\|\bm{\Psi}_{s}(\bm{Z})\|_{\mathbb{X}_{s}}
\displaystyle\leq
\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}C_{0}\left(\epsilon+\|\bm{Z}\|_{\mathbb{X}}^{\min\{p,2\}}\right),
(6.27)
\displaystyle\|\bm{\Psi}_{u}(\bm{Z})\|_{\mathbb{X}_{u}}
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle\|\bm{Z}\|_{\mathbb{X}}^{\min\{p,2\}},
(6.28)
\displaystyle\|\bm{\Psi}(\bm{Z})-\bm{\Psi}(\tilde{\bm{Z}})\|_{\mathbb{X}}
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle\epsilon^{\min\{p-1,1\}}\|\bm{Z}-\tilde{\bm{Z}}\|_{\mathbb{X}}.
(6.29)
Postponing the proof for later steps, we now show that these estimates (6.25)-(6.29) imply Theorem 6.4. Clearly, they imply that \bm{\Psi} is a contraction mapping in B^{\mathbb{X}}_{C_{0}\epsilon} , leading to a global solution Z\in X_{+} solving (1.21) and hence u of the form (6.16) solving (NLS). The unstable-data map \Phi is determined by (Z_{c0},z_{s0})\mapsto z_{u0}:=-\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{\tau A_{u}}f_{u}d\tau , whose contraction estimate (6.19) follows from (6.29) noticing that the \bm{\Psi}_{u} component is defined independent of (Z_{c0},z_{s0}) .
For the estimate of \epsilon (6.17), we combine the boundedness of \bm{Z} with regularity of eigenspaces (6.14) to see
\|e^{\mu\tau}Z\|_{S}\lesssim\|e^{\mu\tau}Z_{c}\|_{S}+\|e^{\mu\tau}z_{s}\|_{L^{2}_{\tau}}+\|e^{\mu\tau}z_{u}\|_{L^{2}_{\tau}}\lesssim\epsilon.
(6.30)
Noticing that \tau(t)=-\frac{\ln(1-2bt)}{2b} , \mu=b(\sigma-s_{c}) and hence e^{\mu\tau}=(1-2bt)^{-\frac{\sigma-s_{c}}{2}} , this is the boundedness of \varepsilon (6.17).
Finally, we check the limit profile in the original coordinates (6.18). From
i\partial_{\tau}Z+({\mathcal{H}}_{b}-ibs_{c})Z=F-VZ,
we have
\displaystyle e^{-i\tau({\mathcal{H}}_{b}-ibs_{c})}Z(\tau)
\displaystyle=
\displaystyle\left(Z(0)-i\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-i\tau^{\prime}({\mathcal{H}}_{b}-ibs_{c})}(F-VZ)(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}\right)
\displaystyle+
\displaystyle i\int_{\tau}^{\infty}e^{-i\tau^{\prime}({\mathcal{H}}_{b}-ibs_{c})}(F-VZ)(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}
\displaystyle=:
\displaystyle Z_{+}+\mathfrak{r}(\tau).
With (6.30), (6.25) and that V:\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}^{\prime}}\to\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}} from Lemma B.1, the Strichartz estimate for e^{it{\mathcal{H}}_{b}} (2.6) implies
\displaystyle\left\|\mathfrak{r}(\tau)\right\|_{\dot{H}^{\sigma}}\lesssim\|e^{\mu\tau}VZ\|_{L^{2}_{\tau}([\tau,\infty),\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}^{\prime}}_{y})}+\|e^{\mu\tau}F\|_{L^{q_{1}^{\prime}}_{\tau}([\tau,\infty),\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}^{\prime}}_{y})}\to 0
as \tau\to+\infty . Thus
e^{b(\sigma-s_{c})\tau}Z(\tau)-e^{i\tau({\mathcal{H}}_{b}-ib\sigma)}Z_{+}=e^{i\tau({\mathcal{H}}_{b}-ib\sigma)}\mathfrak{r}(\tau)\to 0,\quad\mathrm{in}\,\,(\dot{H}^{\sigma})^{2}
as \tau\to+\infty since \|e^{i\tau({\mathcal{H}}_{b}-ib\sigma)}\mathfrak{r}(\tau)\|_{(\dot{H}^{\sigma})^{2}}=\|\mathfrak{r}(\tau)\|_{(\dot{H}^{\sigma})^{2}} . Denote \varepsilon_{+} and \varepsilon_{r} as the first component of Z_{+} and \mathfrak{r} respectively. We invoke (2.4) to go back to the original coordinates
\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda(t)^{\frac{2}{p-1}}}\varepsilon\left(t,\frac{x}{\lambda(t)}\right)e^{i\tau(t)}
\displaystyle=
\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda(t)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\left(e^{i\tau(t)(\Delta_{b}-1)}(\varepsilon_{+}+\varepsilon_{r}(\tau(t))\right)\left(\frac{x}{\lambda(t)}\right)e^{i\tau(t)}
\displaystyle=
\displaystyle e^{it\Delta}\varepsilon_{+}(x)+(e^{it\Delta}\varepsilon_{r}(\tau(t)))(x).
This implies (1.11) with u_{*}:=e^{i\frac{1}{2b}\Delta}\varepsilon_{+} .
Step 2. Onto estimate (6.25)-(6.28).
Case 1. 1<p\leq 2 . We first obtain a spatial nonlinear estimate from the fractional difference estimate Lemma C.1. Note that Q_{b} is non-vanishing (1.6) and satisfies the condition (C.1) with a=1 . Then we have
\|F\|_{\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}^{\prime}}}\lesssim\|Z\|_{L^{pr_{2}^{-}}}^{p}+\|Z\|_{L^{pr_{2}}}^{p-1}\|Z\|_{\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}}}\lesssim\|Z\|_{\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}}\cap\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}^{-}}}^{p}
(6.31)
for 1<p\leq 2 .
Here r^{-}_{i} with i=1,2 are fixed such that 0<r_{i}-r_{i}^{-}\ll 1 and \dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}^{-}}\hookrightarrow L^{pr_{2}^{-}} . We also pick q_{1}^{+} and \beta^{+} such that \frac{2}{q_{1}^{+}}+\frac{d}{r_{1}^{-}}=\frac{d}{2} , \beta^{+}=\frac{1}{q_{1}^{\prime}}-\frac{p}{q_{1}^{+}}.
Next, we verify the nonlinear estimates in the Strichartz norms (6.25). Compute
\displaystyle\|e^{\tilde{p}\mu\tau}F\|_{L^{q_{1}^{\prime}}_{\tau}\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}^{\prime}}_{y}}\lesssim\left\|e^{\tilde{p}\mu\tau}\left(\|Z\|_{\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}}_{y}}^{p}+\|Z\|_{\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}^{-}}_{y}}^{p}\right)\right\|_{L^{q_{1}^{\prime}}_{\tau}}
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle\left\|e^{\tilde{p}\mu\tau}\left(\|Z_{c}\|_{\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}}_{y}}^{p}+\|Z_{c}\|_{\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}^{-}}_{y}}^{p}+|z_{u}|^{p}+|z_{s}|^{p}\right)\right\|_{L^{q_{1}^{\prime}}_{\tau}}
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle\|e^{-(p\mu-\tilde{p}\mu)\tau}\|_{L^{q_{1}^{\prime}}_{\tau}}\left(\|e^{\mu\tau}z_{s}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\tau}}^{p}+\|e^{\mu\tau}z_{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\tau}}^{p}\right)+\|e^{-(p\mu-\tilde{p}\mu)\tau}\|_{L^{\frac{1}{\beta}}_{\tau}}\|e^{\mu\tau}Z_{c}\|_{L^{q_{1}}_{\tau}\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}}_{y}}^{p}
\displaystyle+
\displaystyle\|e^{-(p\mu-\tilde{p}\mu)\tau}\|_{L^{\frac{1}{\beta^{+}}}_{\tau}}\|e^{\mu\tau}Z_{c}\|_{L^{q_{1}^{+}}_{\tau}\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}^{-}}_{y}}^{p}
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle\|e^{\mu\tau}Z_{c}\|_{S}^{p}+\|e^{\mu\tau}z_{s}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\tau}}^{p}+\|e^{\mu\tau}z_{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\tau}}^{p}\lesssim\|\bm{Z}\|_{\mathbb{X}}^{p}
where the second inequality follows (6.14) and that r_{1}\in(2,p_{0}) .
Finally, we check (6.26)-(6.28). For (6.26), we apply the Strichartz estimate (1.29) from Theorem 1.6 to see
\displaystyle\|\bm{\Psi}_{c}(\bm{Z})\|_{\mathbb{X}_{c}}\lesssim\|Z_{c0}\|_{\dot{H}^{\sigma}_{y}}+\|e^{\mu\tau}F\|_{L^{q_{1}^{\prime}}_{\tau}\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}^{\prime}}_{y}}\lesssim\|Z_{c0}\|_{\dot{H}^{\sigma}_{y}}+\|\bm{Z}\|_{\mathbb{X}}^{p}
Then (6.26) holds if we choose C_{0} larger than twice the constant here.
For (6.27), Note that (6.13) and \delta_{1}\geq 2(\sigma-s_{c}) indicate \|e^{-A_{s}\tau}\|_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{s}}\to\mathbb{R}^{d_{s}}}\lesssim e^{-\frac{\tilde{p}+1}{2}\mu\tau} . By Duhamel’s formula for z_{s} -evolution in (6.12) and (6.14), we have
\displaystyle|z_{s}(\tau)|
\displaystyle\leq
\displaystyle\left|e^{-A_{s}\tau}z_{s0}\right|+\left|\int_{0}^{\tau}e^{-A_{s}(\tau-\tau^{\prime})}f_{s}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}\right|
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle e^{-\frac{\tilde{p}+1}{2}\mu\tau}\left(|z_{s0}|+\|e^{\frac{\tilde{p}+1}{2}\mu\tau^{\prime}}\|F(\tau^{\prime})\|_{\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}^{\prime}}_{y}}\|_{L^{1}_{\tau^{\prime}}([0,\tau])}\right)
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle e^{-\frac{\tilde{p}+1}{2}\mu\tau}\left(|z_{s0}|+\|e^{-\frac{\tilde{p}-1}{2}\mu\tau^{\prime}}\|_{L^{q_{1}}_{\tau^{\prime}}}\|e^{\tilde{p}\mu\tau}F\|_{L^{q_{1}^{\prime}}_{\tau}\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}^{\prime}}_{y}}\right),
which implies (6.27) by further applying (6.25) and increasing C_{0} if necessary. Similarly, the estimate of z_{u} (6.28) follows \|e^{A_{u}\tau}\|_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{u}}\to\mathbb{R}^{d_{u}}}\lesssim e^{\frac{\tilde{p}-1}{2}\mu\tau} from (6.13) and the nonlinear estimate (6.25) that
\displaystyle|z_{u}(\tau)|
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle e^{-\frac{\tilde{p}-1}{2}\mu\tau}\left\|e^{\frac{\tilde{p}-1}{2}\mu\tau^{\prime}}\|F(\tau^{\prime})\|_{\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}^{\prime}}_{y}}\right\|_{L^{1}_{\tau^{\prime}}([\tau,\infty))}
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle e^{-\frac{\tilde{p}-1}{2}\mu\tau}\|e^{-\frac{\tilde{p}+1}{2}\mu\tau}\|_{L^{q_{1}}_{\tau^{\prime}}[\tau,\infty)}\|e^{\tilde{p}\mu\tau^{\prime}}F\|_{L^{q_{1}^{\prime}}_{\tau}\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}^{\prime}}_{y}}\lesssim e^{-\tilde{p}\mu\tau}\|\bm{Z}\|_{\mathbb{X}}^{p}.
Case 2. p>2 . We only sketch the proof since the argument is similar. Like (6.31), we have a similar nonlinear estimate
\|F\|_{\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}^{\prime}}}\lesssim\|Z\|_{\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}}\cap\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}^{-}}}^{p}+\|Z\|_{\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}}\cap\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}^{-}}}^{2}
(6.32)
for p>2 from Lemma C.1. To deal with the additional quadratic terms, we introduce \beta_{1}:=\frac{1}{q_{1}^{\prime}}-\frac{2}{q_{1}}>\frac{1}{q_{1}}-\frac{p}{q_{1}}=\beta , and correspondingly \beta_{1}^{+}=\frac{1}{q_{1}^{\prime}}-\frac{2}{q_{1}^{+}} .
Then noticing that \tilde{p}=\frac{3}{2} for p>2 , the weighted nonlinear estimate (6.25) becomes
\displaystyle\|e^{\tilde{p}\mu\tau}F\|_{L^{q_{1}^{\prime}}_{\tau}\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}^{\prime}}_{y}}
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle\|e^{-(p\mu-\tilde{p}\mu)\tau}\|_{L^{q_{1}^{\prime}}_{\tau}}\left(\|e^{\mu\tau}z_{s}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\tau}}^{p}+\|e^{\mu\tau}z_{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\tau}}^{p}\right)+\|e^{-(p\mu-\tilde{p}\mu)\tau}\|_{L^{\frac{1}{\beta}}_{\tau}}\|e^{\mu\tau}Z_{c}\|_{L^{q_{1}}_{\tau}\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}}_{y}}^{p}
\displaystyle+
\displaystyle\|e^{-(p\mu-\tilde{p}\mu)\tau}\|_{L^{\frac{1}{\beta^{+}}}_{\tau}}\|e^{\mu\tau}Z_{c}\|_{L^{q_{1}^{+}}_{\tau}\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}^{-}}_{y}}^{p}
\displaystyle+
\displaystyle\|e^{-(2\mu-\tilde{p}\mu)\tau}\|_{L^{q_{1}^{\prime}}_{\tau}}\left(\|e^{\mu\tau}z_{s}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\tau}}^{2}+\|e^{\mu\tau}z_{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\tau}}^{2}\right)+\|e^{-(2\mu-\tilde{p}\mu)\tau}\|_{L^{\frac{1}{\beta_{1}}}_{\tau}}\|e^{\mu\tau}Z_{c}\|_{L^{q_{1}}_{\tau}\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}}_{y}}^{2}
\displaystyle+
\displaystyle\|e^{-(2\mu-\tilde{p}\mu)\tau}\|_{L^{\frac{1}{\beta_{1}^{+}}}_{\tau}}\|e^{\mu\tau}Z_{c}\|_{L^{q_{1}^{+}}_{\tau}\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}^{-}}_{y}}^{2}
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle\|\bm{Z}\|_{\mathbb{X}}^{p}+\|\bm{Z}\|_{\mathbb{X}}^{2}\lesssim\|\bm{Z}\|_{\mathbb{X}}^{2}.
The other estimates (6.26)-(6.28) follow verbatim as above plus this estimate.
Step 3. Contraction estimate (6.29).
The proof is mainly a nonlinear estimate for the difference flow of (6.12).
Without loss of generality, we assume \varepsilon\geq\tilde{\varepsilon} . We also employ notations \tilde{F}=F(\tilde{Z}) and \triangle F=\tilde{F}-F .
Since the unit ball in L^{q_{1}^{\prime}}_{\tau}([0,\infty),(\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}^{\prime}}_{y})^{2}) is closed in L^{q_{1}}_{\tau}([0,\infty),(L^{r_{2}}_{y})^{2}) and e^{\tilde{p}\delta\tau}\triangle F\in B_{1}^{L^{q_{1}^{\prime}}_{\tau}\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}^{\prime}}_{y}} from (6.25) and \epsilon\leq\epsilon_{0}\ll 1 , the inverse mapping theorem then implies
\left\|e^{\tilde{p}\delta\tau}\triangle F\right\|_{L^{q_{1}^{\prime}}_{\tau}\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}^{\prime}}_{y}}\sim\left\|e^{\tilde{p}\delta\tau}\triangle F\right\|_{L^{q_{1}^{\prime}}_{\tau}L^{r_{2}}_{y}}.
Note that Lemma C.3 indicates
\displaystyle\|\triangle F\|_{L^{r_{2}}}\lesssim\|\triangle Z\|_{L^{pr_{2}}}\left(\|Z\|_{L^{pr_{2}}}^{p-1}+\|\tilde{Z}\|_{L^{pr_{2}}}^{p-1}\right)\lesssim\|\triangle Z\|_{\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}}}\left(\|Z\|_{\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}}}^{p-1}+\|\tilde{Z}\|_{\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}}}^{p-1}\right)
for 1<p\leq 2 and
\displaystyle\|\triangle F\|_{L^{r_{2}}}\lesssim\|\triangle Z\|_{\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}}}\left(\|Z\|_{\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}}}^{p-1}+\|\tilde{Z}\|_{\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}}}^{p-1}+\|Z\|_{\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}}}+\|\tilde{Z}\|_{\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}}}\right)
for p>2 . Similar to Step 2, we can derive
\displaystyle\left\|e^{\tilde{p}\delta\tau}\triangle F\right\|_{L^{q_{1}^{\prime}}_{\tau}\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}^{\prime}}_{y}}\lesssim\|\triangle\bm{Z}\|_{\mathbb{X}}\left(\|\bm{Z}\|_{\mathbb{X}}^{{\overline{p}}-1}+\|\tilde{\bm{Z}}\|_{\mathbb{X}}^{{\overline{p}}-1}\right)\lesssim\epsilon^{{\overline{p}}-1}\|\triangle\bm{Z}\|_{\mathbb{X}}
where {\overline{p}}:=\min\{p,2\} , and thereafter, the linear estimates of Strichartz, e^{-A_{s}\tau} and e^{A_{u}\tau} imply the contraction estimate (6.29) as in Step 1.
∎
6.3. Asymptotic stability in \dot{H}^{\sigma}
In this subsection, we will prove the nonlinear asymptotic stability in \dot{H}^{\sigma} by assuming mode stability Assumption 1.2 by a Brouwer argument for matching initial data.
We begin by computing the eigenpairs generated by symmetry.
Define
\xi_{0}=i\left(\begin{array}[]{c}Q_{b}\\
-{\overline{Q}}_{b}\end{array}\right),\,\,\xi_{1}=\frac{1}{2}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\Lambda Q_{b}\\
\overline{\Lambda Q_{b}}\end{array}\right);\,\,\zeta_{j}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\partial_{j}Q_{b}\\
\overline{\partial_{j}Q_{b}}\end{array}\right),\,\,1\leq j\leq d.
(6.33)
where \Lambda=\frac{2}{p-1}+x\cdot\nabla .
Then \xi_{0},\xi_{1},\zeta_{j}\in X_{+} from their symmetry and the decay of Q_{b} (1.7).
By differentiating the self-similar profile equation (1.5) with respect to phase rotation, scaling and spatial translation, we obtain the algebraic relations
\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}_{b}\xi_{0}=0,\quad{\mathcal{H}}_{b}\xi_{1}=-2bi\xi_{1}-i\xi_{0};\quad{\mathcal{H}}_{b}\zeta_{j}=-bi\zeta_{j},\quad 1\leq j\leq d.
Thus
{\mathcal{H}}_{b}\xi_{0}=0,\quad({\mathcal{H}}_{b}+2bi)\left(\xi_{0}+2b\xi_{1}\right)=0,\quad({\mathcal{H}}_{b}+bi)\zeta_{j}=0.
(6.34)
Proof of theorem 1.4.
Let \epsilon_{1}\leq\epsilon_{0} to be determined later, with \epsilon_{0} from Theorem 6.4. The explicit eigen relations (6.34) and mode stability Assumption 1.2 imply that
P_{u}X_{+}={\rm span}_{\mathbb{R}}(\xi_{0},\xi_{1},\zeta_{1},...,\zeta_{d}).
(6.35)
Step 1. Reduction to matching initial data.
Fix \varepsilon_{0}\in B^{\dot{H}^{\sigma}}_{\epsilon_{1}} . For \vec{\alpha}=(\lambda_{0},x_{0},\theta_{0}) , we define the modulated profile w_{0}^{\vec{\alpha}} to satisfy
(Q_{b}+\varepsilon_{0})(x)=\left(Q_{b}+w^{(\vec{\alpha})}_{0}\right)\left(\frac{x-x_{0}}{\lambda_{0}}\right)\lambda_{0}^{-\frac{d}{2}+s_{c}}e^{-i\theta_{0}},
namely
w^{(\vec{\alpha})}_{0}(y):=\left(Q_{b}+\varepsilon_{0}\right)\left(\lambda_{0}y+x_{0}\right)\lambda_{0}^{\frac{d}{2}-s_{c}}e^{i\theta_{0}}-Q_{b}(y).
(6.36)
We claim that for \epsilon_{1} small enough, there exists C_{*}>0 , such that there exists \vec{\alpha}\in B^{\mathbb{R}^{d+2}}_{C_{*}\|\varepsilon_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{\sigma}}} satisfying
\Phi\left((1-P_{u})\Pi^{-1}w^{(\vec{\alpha})}_{0}\right)=P_{u}\Pi^{-1}w^{(\vec{\alpha})}_{0}
(6.37)
where \Phi is the unstable-data map defined in Theorem 6.4.
Now we prove that (6.37) concludes the proof of Theorem 1.4.
Let
\tilde{u}_{0}:=Q_{b}+w^{(\vec{\alpha})}_{0}=Q_{b}+\Pi(1-P_{u})\Pi^{-1}w^{(\vec{\alpha})}_{0}+\Pi\Phi\left((1-P_{u})\Pi^{-1}w^{(\vec{\alpha})}_{0}\right).
Since
\|\Pi(1-P_{u})\Pi^{-1}w^{(\vec{\alpha})}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{\sigma}}\lesssim\|\varepsilon_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{\sigma}}+|\vec{\alpha}|\lesssim\epsilon_{1},
when \epsilon_{1} is small enough, we have \|\Pi(1-P_{u})\Pi^{-1}w^{(\vec{\alpha})}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{\sigma}}\leq\epsilon_{0} and hence Theorem 6.4 implies that \tilde{u}_{0}
generates a self-similar blowup
\tilde{u}(t,x)=\frac{1}{(1-2bt)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}}(Q_{b}+w)\left(t,\frac{x}{\sqrt{1-2bt}}\right)e^{-i\frac{\ln(\frac{1}{2b}-t)}{2b}},\quad w\big|_{t=0}=w^{(\vec{\alpha})}_{0},
with w satisfies \|w(t)\|_{\dot{H}^{\sigma}_{x}}\lesssim(1-2bt)^{\frac{\sigma-s_{c}}{2}} and there exists a blowup profile \tilde{u}_{*} in the sense of (6.18).
Then from the symmetry of (NLS), the solution generated by u_{0}=Q_{b}+\varepsilon_{0} becomes
u(t,x)=\tilde{u}\left(\frac{t}{\lambda_{0}^{2}},\frac{x-x_{0}}{\lambda_{0}}\right)\lambda_{0}^{-\frac{d}{2}+s_{c}}e^{-i\theta_{0}},
so (1.14) holds with \varepsilon(t)=w(\lambda_{0}^{-2}t) has desired decaying when t\to T:=\frac{\lambda_{0}^{2}}{2b} . The vanishing of \varepsilon(t) implies the uniqueness of the asymptotics decomposition (1.14), and hence the uniqueness of the modulation parameter \vec{\alpha} . The blowup profile for u is u_{*}=\tilde{u}_{*}\left(\frac{\cdot-x_{0}}{\lambda_{0}}\right)\lambda_{0}^{-\frac{d}{2}+s_{c}}e^{-i\theta_{0}} .
Step 2. Parameterization and Brouwer argument.
Now we prove the claim by solving (6.37).
Let
r_{\vec{\alpha}}:=Q_{b}(\lambda_{0}\cdot+x_{0})\lambda_{0}^{\frac{d}{2}-s_{c}}e^{i\theta_{0}}-Q_{b},\quad v_{\vec{\alpha}}=w^{(\vec{\alpha})}_{0}-r_{\vec{\alpha}}=\varepsilon_{0}(\lambda_{0}\cdot+x_{0})\lambda_{0}^{\frac{d}{2}-s_{c}}e^{i\theta_{0}},
then (6.37) becomes
P_{u}\Pi^{-1}r_{\vec{\alpha}}=\Phi\left((1-P_{u})\Pi^{-1}w^{(\vec{\alpha})}_{0}\right)-P_{u}\Pi^{-1}v_{\vec{\alpha}}.
(6.38)
Since P_{u}\big|_{P_{u}X_{+}}=I\big|_{P_{u}X_{+}} , we compute
P_{u}\Pi^{-1}\left(\left(\partial_{\theta_{0}},\partial_{\lambda_{0}},\partial_{(x_{0})_{1}},...,\partial_{(x_{0})_{d}}\right)r_{\vec{\alpha}}\right)\Big|_{\vec{\alpha}=\vec{0}}=\left(\xi_{0},2\xi_{2},\zeta_{1},...,\zeta_{d}\right).
So with (6.35), we can parameterize P_{u}X_{+} by
{\mathcal{P}}:P_{u}X_{+}\to\mathbb{R}^{d+2},\quad c_{1}\xi_{0}+2c_{2}\xi_{1}+\sum_{j=1}^{d}c_{j+2}\zeta_{j}\mapsto\vec{c},
which is clearly well-defined by the linear independence of those modes (6.33). Then {\mathcal{P}}P_{u}\Pi^{-1}(\nabla_{\vec{\alpha}}r_{\vec{\alpha}})\big|_{\vec{\alpha}=\vec{0}}={\rm Id}_{\mathbb{R}^{d+2}} and (6.38) turns into
\begin{split}\vec{\alpha}={\mathcal{T}}_{\varepsilon_{0}}(\vec{\alpha}):&=-{\mathcal{P}}P_{u}\Pi^{-1}\left(r_{\vec{\alpha}}-\vec{\alpha}\cdot(\nabla_{\vec{\alpha}}r_{\vec{\alpha}})\big|_{\vec{\alpha}=\vec{0}}\right)\\
&+{\mathcal{P}}\left[\Phi\left((1-P_{u})\Pi^{-1}w^{(\vec{\alpha})}_{0}\right)-P_{u}\Pi^{-1}v_{\vec{\alpha}}\right].\end{split}
(6.39)
From the boundedness of P_{u} , \Pi^{-1} , {\mathcal{P}} and (6.19), together with that for |\vec{\alpha}|\leq 1 ,
\left\|v_{\vec{\alpha}}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{\sigma}}\lesssim\|\varepsilon_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{\sigma}},\quad\|r_{\vec{\alpha}}\|_{\dot{H}^{\sigma}}\lesssim|\vec{\alpha}|,\quad\|r_{\vec{\alpha}}-\vec{\alpha}\cdot(\nabla_{\vec{\alpha}}r_{\vec{\alpha}})\big|_{\vec{\alpha}=\vec{0}}\|_{\dot{H}^{\sigma}}\lesssim|\vec{\alpha}|^{2},
we have for |\vec{\alpha}|\leq 1 , there exists C_{*}>0 independent of \varepsilon_{0},\vec{\alpha} such that
\displaystyle\left|{\mathcal{T}}_{\varepsilon_{0}}(\vec{\alpha})\right|
\displaystyle\leq
\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}C_{*}\left(\|\varepsilon_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{\sigma}}+|\vec{\alpha}|^{\min\{p,2\}}\right).
Suppose
\epsilon_{1}<(2C_{*}^{\min\{p,2\}})^{\frac{1}{\min\{p-1,1\}}},
then with \|\varepsilon_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{\sigma}}\leq\epsilon_{1} , we have
{\mathcal{T}}_{\varepsilon_{0}}\left(B^{\mathbb{R}^{d+2}}_{C_{*}\|\varepsilon_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{\sigma}}}\right)\subset B^{\mathbb{R}^{d+2}}_{C_{*}\|\varepsilon_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{\sigma}}},
and the Brouwer fixed point theorem implies the existence of solution \vec{\alpha} to (6.39) and hence (6.37). That concludes the proof.
∎
7. Nonlinear stability in H^{1}
7.1. Modified spectral projections
In this subsection, we apply the strategy from [38, Section 2.5]. Our goal is to construct the modified spectral projections by truncating {\rm Ran}P_{\rm disc} .
Lemma 7.1 (Modified spectral projections).
Under the assumption of Lemma 6.3 and assuming additionally (1.8) for Q_{b} , there exist linear operators \tilde{P}_{c},\tilde{P}_{s},\tilde{P}_{u}\in{\mathcal{L}}((\dot{H}^{\sigma})^{2}) satisfying
(1)
I=\tilde{P}_{c}+\tilde{P}_{s}+\tilde{P}_{u} ; for \alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\in\{s,u,c\} , \tilde{P}_{\alpha}\tilde{P}_{\alpha^{\prime}}=\tilde{P}_{\alpha}\delta_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}} .
(2)
For \alpha\in\{s,u\} , \dim({\rm Ran}\tilde{P}_{\alpha})=\dim({\rm Ran}P_{\alpha})<\infty , and {\rm Ran}\tilde{P}_{\alpha}\subset C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{d}) .
(3)
\tilde{P}_{s},\tilde{P}_{u}\in{\mathcal{L}}\left((\dot{W}^{\sigma,p_{1}}+\dot{W}^{\sigma,p_{0}^{\prime}})^{2}\to H^{N}\right) for any N\geq 0 with p_{0} p_{1} as in Lemma 6.3.
(4)
{\mathcal{J}} -invariance: {\mathcal{J}}\circ\tilde{P}_{\alpha}=\tilde{P}_{\alpha}\circ{\mathcal{J}} for \alpha\in\{s,u,c\} .
(5)
Composition with original spectral projections:
\displaystyle\tilde{P}_{\alpha}P_{\alpha}=\tilde{P}_{\alpha},
\displaystyle P_{\alpha}\tilde{P}_{\alpha}=P_{\alpha},\quad{\rm for}\,\,\alpha\in\{s,u\};
(7.1)
\displaystyle\tilde{P}_{c}P_{c}=P_{c},
\displaystyle P_{c}\tilde{P}_{c}=\tilde{P}_{c}.
(7.2)
In particular, {\rm Ran}P_{c}={\rm Ran}\tilde{P}_{c} .
(6)
Action of {\mathcal{H}} on stable/unstable subspaces: View \tilde{P}_{\alpha}{\mathcal{H}}P_{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{L}}({\rm Ran}\tilde{P}_{\alpha}) as matrix operators for \alpha\in\{s,u\} , then
\Im\left(\mathrm{eig}(\tilde{P}_{s}{\mathcal{H}}P_{s})\right)\geq b\delta_{1},\quad\Im\left(\mathrm{eig}(\tilde{P}_{u}{\mathcal{H}}P_{u})\right)\leq 0.
(7.3)
Proof.
Within this proof, we will write the inner product (\cdot,\cdot):=(\cdot,\cdot)_{(\dot{H}^{\sigma})^{2}} for simplicity.
Step 1. Symmetry of discrete spectrum. In this step, we will show that for \alpha\in\{u,s\} , there exists a set of functions \{\psi_{\alpha;j},\varphi_{\alpha;j}\}_{j=1}^{d_{\alpha}}\subset X_{+}\cap(\dot{W}^{\sigma,p_{0}}\cap\dot{W}^{\sigma,p_{1}^{\prime}})^{2} such that
P_{\alpha}=\sum_{j=1}^{d_{\alpha}}(\cdot,\psi_{\alpha;j})\varphi_{\alpha;j},\quad(\varphi_{\alpha;i},\psi_{\alpha;j})=\delta_{ij}.
(7.4)
For simplicity, we only prove the case for \alpha=u .
To begin with, we recall the contour integral definition of P_{u} (6.10) and decompose
P_{u}=P_{\Im}+\sum_{\pm}P_{\Re\pm}
by choosing contour \gamma_{\Im}\subset\mathbb{C} , \gamma_{\Re\pm}\subset\{z\in\mathbb{C}:\pm\Re z>0\} respectively which encompass the unstable modes {\mathcal{E}}_{u}\cap i\mathbb{R} , {\mathcal{E}}_{u}\cap\{z\in\mathbb{C}:\pm\Re z>0\} respectively. Then P_{\Im},P_{\Re\pm} are orthogonal Riesz projections, namely
P_{\beta}P_{\beta^{\prime}}=P_{\beta}\delta_{\beta,\beta^{\prime}}\quad{\rm for}\,\,\beta,\beta^{\prime}\in\{\Im,\Re+,\Re-\}.
(7.5)
Moreover, we can require the symmetry with respect to i\mathbb{R} that \gamma_{\Im}=-{\overline{\gamma}}_{\Im} and \gamma_{\Re\pm}=-{\overline{\gamma}}_{\Re\mp} as sets. Thereafter, a similar computation as in Lemma 6.3 leads to
{\mathcal{J}}P_{\Im}=P_{\Im}{\mathcal{J}},\quad{\mathcal{J}}P_{\Re\pm}=P_{\Re\mp}{\mathcal{J}}.
(7.6)
Next, we claim that we can pick a basis \{v_{j}\}_{j=1}^{M_{1}}\subset X_{+} for {\rm Ran}P_{\Im}\big|_{(\dot{H}^{\sigma})^{2}} . Indeed, from Lemma 6.1, we have ({\mathcal{H}}-\lambda)^{n}\varphi_{\lambda}=0 if and only if ({\mathcal{H}}+{\overline{\lambda}})^{n}{\mathcal{J}}\varphi_{\lambda}=0 . Because P_{\Im}=\sum_{\lambda\in{\mathcal{E}}_{u}\cap i\mathbb{R}}P_{\lambda} , we see \varphi_{\lambda}\in{\rm Ran}P_{\Im}\big|_{(\dot{H}^{\sigma})^{2}} implies {\mathcal{J}}\varphi_{\lambda}\in{\rm Ran}P_{\Im}\big|_{(\dot{H}^{\sigma})^{2}} and thus \varphi_{\lambda}+{\mathcal{J}}\varphi_{\lambda},i(\varphi_{\lambda}-{\mathcal{J}}\varphi_{\lambda})\in{\rm Ran}P_{\Im}\big|_{(\dot{H}^{\sigma})^{2}} . Since the last vectors belongs to X_{+} , the choice of \{v_{j}\} follows.
Now we also take \{w_{j}\}_{j=1}^{M_{2}} to be the basis of {\rm Ran}P_{\Re+}\big|_{(\dot{H}^{\sigma})^{2}} . Then there exists \{h_{j}\}_{j=1}^{M_{1}}\subset{\rm Ran}(P_{\Im}\big|_{(\dot{H}^{\sigma})^{2}})^{*} , \{g_{j}\}_{j=1}^{M_{2}}\subset{\rm Ran}(P_{\Re+}\big|_{(\dot{H}^{\sigma})^{2}})^{*} such that
P_{\Im}=\sum_{j=1}^{M_{1}}(\cdot,h_{j})v_{j},\quad P_{\Re+}=\sum_{j=1}^{M_{2}}(\cdot,g_{j})w_{j}.
The symmetry (7.6) indicates that
P_{\Re-}={\mathcal{J}}P_{\Re+}{\mathcal{J}}=\sum_{j=1}^{M_{2}}\overline{({\mathcal{J}}\cdot,g_{j})}{\mathcal{J}}w_{j}=\sum_{j=1}^{M_{2}}(\cdot,{\mathcal{J}}g_{j}){\mathcal{J}}w_{j},
and that h_{j}={\mathcal{J}}h_{j} , namely h_{j}\in X_{+} . The projection property (7.5) then implies
(v_{i},h_{j})=(w_{i},g_{j})=\delta_{ij},\quad(v_{i},g_{j})=(w_{i},h_{j})=({\mathcal{J}}w_{i},g_{j})=0,\quad\forall i,j.
(7.7)
Therefore we can compute
\displaystyle P_{\alpha}
\displaystyle=
\displaystyle P_{\Im}+P_{\Re+}+P_{\Re-}=\sum_{j=1}^{M_{1}}(\cdot,h_{j})v_{j}+\sum_{j=1}^{M_{2}}\left[(\cdot,g_{j})w_{j}+(\cdot,{\mathcal{J}}g_{j}){\mathcal{J}}w_{j}\right]
\displaystyle=
\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{M_{1}}(\cdot,h_{j})v_{j}+\sum_{j=1}^{M_{2}}\left[(\cdot,g_{j}+{\mathcal{J}}g_{j})\frac{w_{j}+{\mathcal{J}}w_{j}}{2}+(\cdot,i(g_{j}-{\mathcal{J}}g_{j}))i\frac{w_{j}-{\mathcal{J}}w_{j}}{2}\right].
This clearly implies the choice of \{\psi_{u;j},\varphi_{u;j}\}_{j=1}^{d_{u}}\subset X_{+} with d_{u}=M_{1}+2M_{2} to ensure the representation formula in (7.4), and the orthogonal conditions follow from (7.7). Finally, the additional regularity \psi_{u;j},\varphi_{u;j}\in(\dot{W}^{\sigma,p_{0}}\cap\dot{W}^{\sigma,p_{1}^{\prime}})^{2} was verified in Proposition 4.5.
Step 2. Definition and properties of modified spectral projections. For R>0 we define the matrix by
M_{\alpha;R}=\{(\chi_{R}\varphi_{\alpha;i},\psi_{\alpha;j})\}_{1\leq i,j\leq d_{\alpha}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\alpha}\times d_{\alpha}},\quad{\rm for}\,\,\alpha\in\{u,s\}.
(7.8)
Since \varphi_{\alpha;i},\psi_{\alpha;j}\in(\dot{H}^{\sigma})^{2} , we have M_{\alpha;R}=I_{d_{\alpha}}+o_{R}(1) . So we can fix a R_{0}\gg 1 , such that M_{u;R_{0}} , M_{s;R_{0}} are invertible, and define for \alpha\in\{u,s\} that
\left(\tilde{\varphi}_{\alpha;1},...,\tilde{\varphi}_{\alpha;d_{\alpha}}\right)^{\top}=M_{\alpha;R_{0}}^{-1}\left(\chi_{R_{0}}\varphi_{\alpha;1},...,\chi_{R_{0}}\varphi_{\alpha;d_{\alpha}}\right)^{\top},
(7.9)
and
\tilde{P}_{\alpha}=\sum_{j=1}^{d_{\alpha}}(\cdot,\psi_{\alpha;j})_{(\dot{H}^{\sigma})^{2}}\tilde{\varphi}_{\alpha;j},\quad{\rm for\,\,}\alpha\in\{u,s\};\quad\tilde{P}_{c}=I-\tilde{P}_{s}-\tilde{P}_{u}.
(7.10)
From the orthogonality (7.4) and definition of \tilde{\varphi}_{\alpha;j} (7.9), we have
(\tilde{\varphi}_{\alpha;i},\psi_{\alpha;j})=\delta_{ij}
(7.11)
which implies the projection property (1). With Q_{b}\in C^{\infty}_{loc} from (1.8), standard elliptic interior regularity theory implies that {\rm Ran}P_{s}\cup{\rm Ran}P_{u}\subset C^{\infty}_{loc} . Thus {\rm Ran}\tilde{P}_{\alpha}=\chi_{R_{0}}{\rm Ran}P_{\alpha}\subset C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{d}) for \alpha\in\{u,s\} and it concludes (2) and (3). (4) follows from \chi_{R_{0}}\varphi_{\alpha;j}\in X_{+} .
For (5), (7.1) follows a direct computation using the definition (7.9) and the orthogonality conditions (7.4), (7.11), while (7.2) comes from (7.1) and \tilde{P}_{\alpha}P_{\alpha^{\prime}}=P_{\alpha}\tilde{P}_{\alpha^{\prime}}=0 for (\alpha,\alpha^{\prime})=(u,s) or (s,u) . For (6), it suffices to observe that \tilde{P}_{\alpha}{\mathcal{H}}P_{\alpha} under the basis \{\tilde{\varphi}_{\alpha;j}\} has the same matrix representation as {\mathcal{H}}\big|_{{\rm Ran}P_{\alpha}} under the basis \{\varphi_{\alpha;j}\} by definition.
∎
Similar to (6.11) and (6.12), we let
\displaystyle\tilde{Z}_{\alpha}=\tilde{P}_{\alpha}Z,\quad\tilde{F}_{\alpha}=\tilde{P}_{\alpha}F,\quad\tilde{X}_{\alpha}=\tilde{P}_{\alpha}X_{+},\quad{\rm for\,\,}\alpha\in\{u,s,c\}.
And for \alpha\in\{u,s\} , we define the \mathbb{R} -linear homeomorphism \phi_{\alpha}:\tilde{X}_{\alpha}\to\mathbb{R}^{d_{\alpha}} as parametrization,
\tilde{z}_{\alpha}=\tilde{\phi}_{\alpha}(\tilde{Z}_{\alpha}),\quad\tilde{z}_{\alpha}=\tilde{\phi}_{\alpha}(\tilde{Z}_{\alpha}),\quad\tilde{A}_{\alpha}=\phi_{\alpha}^{-1}\circ(-i\tilde{P}_{\alpha}{\mathcal{H}}P_{\alpha})\circ\phi_{\alpha}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\alpha}\times d_{\alpha}}.
From Lemma 7.1, they satisfy for \alpha\in\{s,u\} and all N\geq 0 and q\in[2,p_{0}] that
\displaystyle\Re\left(\mathrm{eig}(\tilde{A}_{s})\right)\geq b\delta_{1},
\displaystyle\Re\left(\mathrm{eig}(\tilde{A}_{u})\right)\leq 0,
(7.12)
\displaystyle|\tilde{f}_{\alpha}|\sim_{N}\|\tilde{F}_{\alpha}\|_{H^{N}}\lesssim_{N}\|F\|_{\dot{H}^{\sigma}+\dot{W}^{\sigma,p_{0}^{\prime}}},
\displaystyle|\tilde{z}_{\alpha}|\sim_{q,N}\|\tilde{Z}_{\alpha}\|_{\dot{W}^{\sigma,q}\cap W^{1,q}\cap H^{N}_{\langle x\rangle^{N}}}.
(7.13)
Now we compute the modified spectrally decoupled flow. We first compute using (7.1)-(7.2) that
\displaystyle\tilde{P}_{\alpha}{\mathcal{H}}Z=\tilde{P}_{\alpha}P_{\alpha}{\mathcal{H}}Z=\tilde{P}_{\alpha}{\mathcal{H}}P_{\alpha}Z=\tilde{P}_{\alpha}{\mathcal{H}}P_{\alpha}\tilde{P}_{\alpha}Z=\tilde{P}_{\alpha}{\mathcal{H}}P_{\alpha}\tilde{Z}_{\alpha},\quad\alpha\in\{u,s\},
\displaystyle\tilde{P}_{c}{\mathcal{H}}Z=\tilde{P}_{c}{\mathcal{H}}(\tilde{Z}_{c}+\tilde{Z}_{s}+\tilde{Z}_{u})={\mathcal{H}}\tilde{Z}_{c}+\tilde{P}_{c}{\mathcal{H}}(\tilde{Z}_{s}+\tilde{Z}_{u})
So applying the modified projection \tilde{P}_{s},\tilde{P}_{u},\tilde{P}_{c} to the self-similar flow (1.21) yields
\left|\begin{array}[]{l}i\partial_{\tau}\tilde{Z}_{c}+{\mathcal{H}}\tilde{Z}_{c}=\tilde{F}_{c}-\tilde{P}_{c}{\mathcal{H}}(\tilde{Z}_{s}+\tilde{Z}_{u})\\
\frac{d}{d\tau}\tilde{z}_{s}+\tilde{A}_{s}\tilde{z}_{s}=\tilde{f}_{s},\\
\frac{d}{d\tau}\tilde{z}_{u}+\tilde{A}_{u}\tilde{z}_{u}=\tilde{f}_{u}.\end{array}\right.
(7.14)
7.2. Strichartz estimate in \dot{H}^{\sigma}\cap\dot{H}^{1}
Lemma 7.2 ( \dot{H}^{\sigma}\cap\dot{H}^{1} Strichartz estimate).
Under the assumption of Lemma 6.3 and assuming additionally (1.8) for Q_{b} ,
the following linear estimate holds for the linear system i\partial_{t}Z+{\mathcal{H}}Z=F
\|P_{\rm ess}Z\|_{\tilde{S}_{\sigma}\cap\tilde{S}_{1}}\lesssim\|P_{\rm ess}Z(0)\|_{\dot{H}^{\sigma}\cap\dot{H}^{1}}+\|P_{\rm ess}F\|_{\tilde{S}_{\sigma}^{\prime}\cap\tilde{S}_{1}^{\prime}}
(7.15)
where
\displaystyle\tilde{S}_{\alpha}
\displaystyle=\left(C^{0}_{e^{\mu\tau}}\dot{H}^{\alpha}\cap L^{q_{1}}_{e^{\mu\tau}}\dot{W}^{\alpha,r_{1}}\cap L^{2}_{e^{\mu\tau}}(\dot{W}^{\alpha,r_{0}}\cap\dot{W}^{\alpha,r_{1}})\right)([0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
\displaystyle\tilde{S}_{\alpha}^{\prime}
\displaystyle=(L^{q_{2}^{\prime}}_{e^{\mu\tau}}+L^{2}_{e^{\mu\tau}})\dot{W}^{\alpha,r_{2}^{\prime}}([0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
with \mu=b(\sigma-s_{c}) , 0<r_{0}-2\ll 1 ; for i=1,2 , r_{i}\in(2,p_{0}] for p_{0} from (6.2), and q_{i}\geq 2 is determined by \frac{2}{q_{i}}+\frac{d}{r_{i}}=\frac{d}{2} .
Proof.
The bound for \tilde{S}_{\sigma} follows from the \dot{H}^{\sigma} Strichartz estiamte (1.29), so it suffices to control \|P_{\rm ess}Z\|_{\tilde{S}_{1}} , which will basically follow from (1.29) plus some commutator estimates and boundedness of spectral projections.
We also note that \|\cdot\|_{D^{1-\sigma}\dot{W}^{\sigma,q}}\sim\|\cdot\|_{\dot{W}^{1,q}} for q\in(1,\infty) , thanks to the L^{q} boundedness of Riesz transform {\mathcal{R}}_{i}:=\partial_{i}D^{-1} and that \sum_{i}^{2}{\mathcal{R}}_{i}=-1 . In particular \tilde{S}_{1}=D^{1-\sigma}\tilde{S}_{\sigma} and \tilde{S}_{1}^{\prime}=D^{1-\sigma}\tilde{S}_{\sigma}^{\prime} .
Firstly, we compute with (2.46) that
D^{1-\sigma}{\mathcal{H}}=\left({\mathcal{H}}_{b}+ib(1-\sigma)+D^{1-\sigma}VD^{-(1-\sigma)}\right)D^{1-\sigma}=\left({\mathcal{H}}_{b}+ib(1-\sigma)\right)D^{1-\sigma}-[V,D^{1-\sigma}].
Therefore applying P_{\rm ess}D^{1-\sigma}P_{\rm ess} the original linear equation (1.21) yields
\displaystyle\left(i\partial_{t}+{\mathcal{H}}+ib(1-\sigma)\right)P_{\rm ess}D^{1-\sigma}Z_{\rm ess}=P_{\rm ess}D^{1-\sigma}F_{\rm ess}+P_{\rm ess}[V,D^{1-\sigma}]Z_{\rm ess}
where we denote Z_{\rm ess}=P_{\rm ess}Z , F_{\rm ess}=P_{\rm ess}F . With \nu=\mu-b(1-\sigma) , the \dot{H}^{\sigma} -Strichartz estimate (1.29) implies
\displaystyle\|P_{\rm ess}D^{1-\sigma}Z_{\rm ess}\|_{\tilde{S}_{\sigma}}
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle\|P_{\rm ess}D^{1-\sigma}Z_{\rm ess}(0)\|_{\dot{H}^{\sigma}}+\|P_{\rm ess}[V,D^{1-\sigma}]Z_{\rm ess}\|_{L^{2}_{\tau}\dot{W}^{\sigma,2_{-}}_{x}}
\displaystyle+
\displaystyle\|e^{\mu t}P_{\rm ess}D^{1-\sigma}F_{\rm ess}\|_{(L^{q_{1}^{\prime}}_{\tau}+L^{2}_{\tau})(\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}^{\prime}}_{x}+\dot{W}^{\sigma,2_{-}}_{x})}
Recall the boundedness of P_{\rm dist} from Proposition 4.5 (3), so that P_{\rm ess}={\rm Id}-P_{\rm dist}\in{\mathcal{L}}(\dot{W}^{\sigma,2_{-}})\cap{\mathcal{L}}(\dot{H}^{\sigma})\cap{\mathcal{L}}(\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}^{\prime}}\to\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}^{\prime}}+\dot{W}^{\sigma,2_{-}}) with 0<2-2_{-}\ll 1 .
Plus the commutator estimate in Lemma B.2 (iv) (where we used |\nabla^{2}V|\lesssim\langle x\rangle^{-4} from (1.8)), with 0<2_{+}-2\ll 1 and control of \|Z_{\rm ess}\|_{L^{2}_{\tau}\dot{W}^{\sigma,2_{+}}_{x}} by Strichartz estimate (1.29), we obtain
\|P_{\rm ess}D^{1-\sigma}Z_{\rm ess}\|_{\tilde{S}_{\sigma}}\lesssim\|Z_{\rm ess}(0)\|_{\dot{H}^{1}}+\|F_{\rm ess}\|_{\tilde{S}_{1}^{\prime}}+\|Z_{\rm ess}\|_{\tilde{S}_{\sigma}}.
(7.16)
Next, we claim that
P_{\rm dist}\circ\chi_{R}\circ D^{1-\sigma}\in{\mathcal{L}}(\dot{H}^{\sigma}+\dot{W}^{\sigma,2_{+}}\to\dot{H}^{\sigma}\cap\dot{W}^{\sigma,p_{0}})
(7.17)
for any R>0 and r\in[2,p_{0}] . Indeed, by rewriting P_{\rm dist}=\sum_{j}(\cdot,\psi_{j})_{\dot{H}^{\sigma}}\varphi_{j} and using that \dot{H}^{\sigma}+\dot{W}^{\sigma,2_{+}}\subset L^{2_{\sigma}}+L^{2_{\sigma+}} with 2_{\sigma}=\frac{2d}{d-2\sigma}\in(2,\infty) , the claim follows that D^{1-\sigma}\chi_{R}D^{2\sigma}\psi_{j}\in L^{1}\cap L^{\infty} and \varphi_{j}\in\dot{H}^{\sigma}\cap\dot{W}^{\sigma,p_{0}} . From Proposition 4.5, the regularity of \varphi_{j} is verified and \psi_{j}\in{\rm Ran}P_{\rm dist}^{*}\subset\dot{W}^{\sigma,p_{1}}\cap\dot{W}^{\sigma,p_{1}^{\prime}} for some p_{1}>2 . We will show that this implies D^{\sigma}\psi_{j}\in C^{\infty}_{loc}\cap L^{2} . Then D^{2\sigma}\psi_{j}\in C^{\infty}_{loc} , and the desired regularity of D^{1-\sigma}\chi_{R}D^{2\sigma}\psi_{j} follows, which concludes (7.17).
Suppose \psi_{j} is an eigenfunction, namely ({\mathcal{H}}^{*}-{\overline{\xi}})\psi_{j}=0 . Since V^{\top}\in C^{\infty}_{loc} and D^{-\sigma}V^{\top}D^{\sigma}\in{\mathcal{L}}(L^{2}) from (B.4), the elliptic regularity theory implies D^{\sigma}\psi_{j}\in H^{2}_{loc} and then D^{2\sigma}\psi_{j}\in C^{\infty}_{loc} exploiting that locality for the equation for D^{2\sigma}\psi_{j} . When \psi_{j} is an generalized eigenfunction ({\mathcal{H}}^{*}-{\overline{\xi}})^{N}\psi_{j}=0 , the smoothness can be similarly shown by induction on N .
Now we are in place to bound the unstable part of D^{1-\sigma}Z_{\rm ess} . Lemma (B.1) indicates that \|(1-\chi_{R})\|_{\dot{W}^{\sigma,(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\sigma}{d}-\frac{1}{q})^{-1}}}\lesssim\|(1-\chi_{R})\|_{\dot{W}^{1,(\frac{1}{d}+\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{q})^{-1}}}\lesssim R^{-d(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{q})} for 2<q<\min\{\frac{2d}{d-2\alpha},\frac{d}{\sigma}\} , so with the regularity of \varphi_{j},\psi_{j} above, we have
\displaystyle\|P_{\rm dist}\circ(1-\chi_{R})\|_{\dot{H}^{\sigma}+\dot{W}^{\sigma,2_{+}}\to\dot{H}^{\sigma}\cap\dot{W}^{\sigma,p_{0}}}
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle\|D^{\sigma}(1-\chi_{R})D^{-\sigma}\|_{L^{2}\to L^{(2_{+})^{\prime}}}+\|D^{\sigma}(1-\chi_{R})D^{-\sigma}\|_{L^{2_{+}}\to L^{2}}=o_{R}(1).
We compute using this and (7.17)
\displaystyle\|P_{\rm dist}D^{1-\sigma}Z_{\rm ess}\|_{\tilde{S}_{\sigma}}\lesssim\|P_{\rm dist}\chi_{R}D^{1-\sigma}Z_{\rm ess}\|_{\tilde{S}_{\sigma}}+\|P_{\rm dist}(1-\chi_{R})D^{1-\sigma}Z_{\rm ess}\|_{\tilde{S}_{\sigma}}
(7.18)
\displaystyle\leq
\displaystyle C_{R}\|e^{\mu\tau}Z_{\rm ess}\|_{C^{0}\dot{H}^{\sigma}\cap(L^{q_{1}}\cap L^{2})\dot{W}^{\sigma,2_{+}}}+o_{R}(1)\|e^{\mu\tau}D^{1-\sigma}Z_{\rm ess}\|_{C^{0}\dot{H}^{\sigma}\cap(L^{q_{1}}\cap L^{2})\dot{W}^{\sigma,2_{+}}}
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle C_{R}\|Z_{\rm ess}\|_{\tilde{S}_{\sigma}}+o_{R}(1)\|Z_{\rm ess}\|_{\tilde{S}_{1}}
Here we requires r_{0}\leq 2_{+} so that C^{0}_{e^{\mu}\tau}\dot{H}^{\sigma}\cap(L^{q_{1}}_{e^{\mu}\tau}\cap L^{2}_{e^{\mu}\tau})\dot{W}^{\sigma,2_{+}}\hookrightarrow\tilde{S}_{\sigma} .
Finally, since \|Z_{\rm ess}\|_{\tilde{S}_{1}}\sim\|D^{1-\sigma}Z_{\rm ess}\|_{\tilde{S}_{\sigma}}\lesssim\|P_{\rm ess}D^{1-\sigma}Z_{\rm ess}\|_{\tilde{S}_{\sigma}}+\|P_{\rm dist}D^{1-\sigma}Z_{\rm ess}\|_{\tilde{S}_{\sigma}} , we can conclude (7.15) by summing up (7.16) and (7.18), taking R\gg 1 to absorb o_{R}(1)\|Z_{\rm ess}\|_{\tilde{S}_{1}} term to the left and applying the \dot{H}^{\sigma} Strichartz (1.29).
∎
7.3. Proof of nonlinear stability in H^{1}
We begin by proving the counterpart of Theorem 6.4. Here we denote
\tilde{X}_{+}:=X_{+}\cap(\dot{H}^{1})^{2}\subset(\dot{H}^{\sigma}\cap\dot{H}^{1})^{2}.
(7.19)
and will still use the natural isometry \Pi:\tilde{X}_{+}\to\dot{H}^{\sigma}\cap\dot{H}^{1} from (6.15).
Theorem 7.3 ( \dot{H}^{\sigma}\cap\dot{H}^{1} finite-codimensional asymptotic stability).
Let d\geq 1 , s_{c}\in(0,1) , and b,Q_{b} satisfying (1.5), (1.6), (1.7), (1.8).
Then for every 0<\sigma-s_{c}\ll 1 , the following statements hold true.
(1)
Finite codimensional stability: There exists \epsilon_{0}\ll 1 and a Lipschitz map
\tilde{\Phi}:B_{\epsilon_{0}}^{\tilde{X}_{+}}\cap\tilde{P}_{cs}\tilde{X}_{+}\to\tilde{P}_{u}\tilde{X}_{+}
such that for all \tilde{\varepsilon}_{cs}\in B_{\epsilon_{0}}^{\dot{H}^{\sigma}\cap\dot{H}^{1}}\cap\Pi\tilde{P}_{cs}\tilde{X}_{+} , the initial data
u_{0}=Q_{b}+\tilde{\varepsilon}_{cs}+\Pi\tilde{\Phi}(\Pi^{-1}\tilde{\varepsilon}_{cs})
generates a solution of (NLS) blowing up at T=\frac{1}{2b} satisfying
\displaystyle u(t,x)
\displaystyle=
\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda(t)^{\frac{2}{p-1}}}(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)\left(t,\frac{x}{\lambda(t)}\right)e^{i\tau(t)},
(7.20)
\displaystyle\|\varepsilon(t)\|_{\dot{H}^{\sigma}_{x}\cap\dot{H}^{1}_{x}}
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle(1-2bt)^{\frac{\sigma-s_{c}}{2}},
(7.21)
where (\lambda(t),\gamma(t),\tau(t)) are given by (1.3),
and there exists u_{*}\in\dot{H}^{\sigma}\cap\dot{H}^{1} such that
u(t)-\frac{1}{\lambda(t)^{\frac{2}{p-1}}}Q_{b}\left(\frac{x}{\lambda(t)}\right)e^{i\tau(t)}\to u_{*}\quad\mathrm{in}\,\,\dot{H}^{\sigma}\cap\dot{H}^{1}\quad as\quad t\to T.
(7.22)
Here \tilde{P}_{u} and \tilde{P}_{cs}:=\tilde{P}_{c}+\tilde{P}_{s} are from Lemma 7.1.
(2)
Contraction of the unstable-data map: \tilde{\Phi} satisfies \tilde{\Phi}(0)=0 and for any N\geq 1 ,
\|\tilde{\Phi}(\varepsilon_{1})-\tilde{\Phi}(\varepsilon_{2})\|_{H^{N}_{\langle x\rangle^{N}}}\lesssim_{N}\left(\|\varepsilon_{1}\|_{\tilde{X}_{+}}+\|\varepsilon_{2}\|_{\tilde{X}_{+}}\right)^{\min\{p-1,1\}}\|\varepsilon_{1}-\varepsilon_{2}\|_{\tilde{X}_{+}}.
(7.23)
Proof.
The proof is almost identical with that of Theorem 6.4. We only sketch the proof and focus on the new elements.
We use the same \delta_{0},p_{0} from (6.2), the same requirement of \delta_{1},\sigma and the same exponents \mu,r_{1},r_{2},q_{1},\beta,\tilde{p} as in (6.20)-(6.22). Now we define the linear map \tilde{\bm{\Psi}}(\tilde{\bm{Z}})=(\tilde{\bm{\Psi}}_{c}(\tilde{\bm{Z}}),\tilde{\bm{\Psi}}_{s}(\tilde{\bm{Z}}),\tilde{\bm{\Psi}}_{u}(\tilde{\bm{Z}})) for the modified flow (7.14) as
\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\tilde{\bm{\Psi}}_{c}(\tilde{\bm{Z}})\\
\tilde{\bm{\Psi}}_{s}(\tilde{\bm{Z}})\\
\tilde{\bm{\Psi}}_{u}(\tilde{\bm{Z}})\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}e^{it{\mathcal{H}}}\tilde{Z}_{c0}-i\int_{0}^{t}e^{i(t-\tau){\mathcal{H}}}\left(\tilde{F}_{c}-\tilde{P}_{c}{\mathcal{H}}(\tilde{Z}_{s}+\tilde{Z}_{u})\right)d\tau\\
e^{-t\tilde{A}_{s}}\tilde{z}_{s0}+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-(t-\tau)\tilde{A}_{s}}\tilde{f}_{s}d\tau\\
-\int_{t}^{\infty}e^{(\tau-t)\tilde{A}_{u}}\tilde{f}_{u}d\tau\end{array}\right)
(7.24)
The Banach space we use here is
\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{X}}(A_{0})=\tilde{S}\times A_{0}\mathbb{X}_{s}\times A_{0}\mathbb{X}_{u},\quad\|\tilde{\bm{Z}}\|_{\tilde{\mathbb{X}}(A_{0})}=\max\{\|\tilde{Z}_{c}\|_{\tilde{S}},A_{0}\|\tilde{z}_{s}\|_{\mathbb{X}_{s}},A_{0}\|\tilde{z}_{u}\|_{\mathbb{X}_{u}}\},
where A_{0}\gg 1 is a constant chosen later, \mathbb{X}_{\alpha}=C^{0}_{e^{\frac{\tilde{p}+1}{2}\mu t}}\left([0,\infty),\mathbb{R}^{d_{\alpha}}\right) for \alpha\in\{s,u\} as before and the Strichartz norm \tilde{S}=S\cap D^{1-\sigma}S with S from (6.24). We remark that this is controlled by the Strichartz norm from Lemma 7.2. The core proof of Theorem 7.3 will again be reduced to proving contraction mapping of \tilde{\bm{\Phi}} in B^{\tilde{\mathbb{X}}(A_{0})}_{C_{0}\epsilon} with \epsilon_{0}\ll 1 , C_{0}\gg 1 and A_{0}\gg 1 , namely the estimates similar to (6.25)-(6.29).
For the nonlinear estimate, the L^{q_{1}^{\prime}}_{e^{\mu t}}\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}^{\prime}} control namely (6.25) still holds, and the L^{q_{1}^{\prime}}_{e^{\mu t}}\dot{W}^{1,r_{1}^{\prime}} control follows from Lemma C.4 that
\displaystyle\|F\|_{\dot{W}^{1,r_{1}^{\prime}}}\lesssim\left|\begin{array}[]{ll}\|Z\|_{L^{pr_{2}^{-}}}+\|Z\|_{L^{pr_{2}}}\|Z\|_{\dot{W}^{1,r_{1}}}\lesssim\|Z\|^{p}_{\dot{W}^{1,r_{1}}\cap\dot{W}^{1,r_{1}^{-}}}&1<p\leq 2,\\
\|Z\|^{p}_{\dot{W}^{1,r_{1}}\cap\dot{W}^{1,r_{1}^{-}}}+\|Z\|^{2}_{\dot{W}^{1,r_{1}}\cap\dot{W}^{1,r_{1}^{-}}}&p>2.\end{array}\right.
Then similar computations in the time-variable with (7.13) implies for any A_{0}\geq 1
\|e^{\tilde{p}\mu\tau}F(Z)\|_{L^{q_{1}^{\prime}}(\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}^{\prime}}\cap\dot{W}^{1,r_{1}^{\prime}})}\lesssim_{A_{0}}\|\bm{Z}\|_{\tilde{\mathbb{X}}(A_{0})}^{\min\{p,2\}}.
The onto estimates of \tilde{z}_{s} , \tilde{z}_{u} hold in the same way as (6.27)-(6.28), thanks to the same linear estimate of e^{-t\tilde{A}_{s}} , e^{t\tilde{A}_{u}} from Lemma 7.1 (6). For the onto estimate of \tilde{Z}_{c} , we first treat the additional source term \tilde{P}_{c}{\mathcal{H}}(\tilde{Z}_{s}+\tilde{Z}_{u}) by exploiting its high regularity (7.13) to see
\|\tilde{P}_{c}{\mathcal{H}}(\tilde{Z}_{s}+\tilde{Z}_{u})\|_{\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}^{\prime}}\cap\dot{W}^{1,r_{1}^{\prime}}}\lesssim|\tilde{z}_{s}|+|\tilde{z}_{u}|,
which implies
\|e^{\mu\tau}\tilde{P}_{c}{\mathcal{H}}(\tilde{Z}_{s}+\tilde{Z}_{u})\|_{L^{q_{1}}(\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}^{\prime}}\cap\dot{W}^{1,r_{1}^{\prime}})}\lesssim\|\tilde{z}_{s}\|_{\mathbb{X}_{s}}+\|\tilde{z}_{u}\|_{\mathbb{X}_{u}}\leq 2A_{0}^{-1}\|\tilde{\bm{Z}}\|_{\tilde{\mathbb{X}}(A_{0})}
(7.26)
thanks to higher exponential decay in \mathbb{X}_{s} and \mathbb{X}_{u} . Therefore, thanks to \tilde{Z}_{c}=P_{c}\tilde{Z}_{c} by Lemma 7.1 (5), Lemma 7.2 provides the counterpart of the linear Strichartz estimate of e^{it{\mathcal{H}}} in \dot{H}^{\sigma}\cap\dot{H}^{1} , implying that
\|\tilde{\bm{\Psi}}_{c}(\tilde{\bm{Z}})\|_{\tilde{S}}\lesssim\|Z_{c0}\|_{\dot{H}^{\sigma}\cap\dot{H}^{1}}+C_{A_{0}}\|\bm{Z}\|_{\tilde{\mathbb{X}}(A_{0})}^{\min\{p,2\}}+A_{0}^{-1}\|\bm{Z}\|_{\tilde{\mathbb{X}}(A_{0})}.
This requires us to choose A_{0} large enough to ensure smallness of \tilde{z}_{s} , \tilde{z}_{u} relatively to \tilde{Z}_{c} (and then letting \epsilon_{0}\ll 1 to absorb the C_{A_{0}} constant by smallness of nonlinearity), so that this concludes the onto estimate for contraction mapping.
Finally, for the contraction estimate, with the inverse mapping theorem argument, we can apply the same nonlinear estimate and (7.26) for additional term to conclude
\displaystyle\|\tilde{\bm{\Psi}}_{\alpha}(\tilde{\bm{Z}})-\tilde{\bm{\Psi}}_{\alpha}(\tilde{\bm{Z}}^{\prime})\|_{\mathbb{X}_{\alpha}}
\displaystyle\lesssim_{A_{0}}
\displaystyle\epsilon^{\min\{p-1,1\}}\|\tilde{\bm{Z}}-\tilde{\bm{Z}}^{\prime}\|_{\tilde{\mathbb{X}}(A_{0})}\quad{\rm for}\,\,\alpha\in\{u,s\};
(7.27)
\displaystyle\|\tilde{\bm{\Psi}}_{c}(\tilde{\bm{Z}})-\tilde{\bm{\Psi}}_{c}(\tilde{\bm{Z}}^{\prime})\|_{\tilde{S}}
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle\left(C(A_{0})\epsilon^{\min\{p-1,1\}}+A_{0}^{-1}\right)\|\tilde{\bm{Z}}-\tilde{\bm{Z}}^{\prime}\|_{\tilde{\mathbb{X}}(A_{0})}.
Thus with A_{0}\ll 1 and \epsilon_{0}\ll 1 , this is the contraction estimate and concludes that \tilde{\bm{\Phi}} is contraction mapping. Following the argument in Step 1 as Theorem 6.4, this implies all statements of Theorem 7.3. We only stress that the Lipschitz estimate (7.23) only depends on the difference estimate (7.27) with \alpha=u , so the smallness is not affected by the new error in \tilde{Z}_{c} evolution. The norm on LHS of (7.23) comes from (7.13).
∎
Finally, we conclude the proof of Theorem 1.5.
Proof of Theorem 1.5.
This theorem is mainly an application of Theorem 1.5. So we fix a 0<\sigma-s_{c}\ll 1 throughout the proof.
To begin with, we construct the first H^{1} blowup. Denote h_{R_{1}} for R_{1}\geq 1 as
h_{R_{1}}=\tilde{P}_{cs}\Pi^{-1}\left((\chi_{R_{1}}-1)Q_{b}\right),
then \|h_{R_{1}}\|_{\tilde{X}_{+}}=o_{R_{1}\to\infty}(1) . Then when R_{1}\gg 1 , we have h_{R_{1}}\in B^{\tilde{X}_{+}}_{\epsilon_{0}}\cap\tilde{P}_{cs}\tilde{X}_{+} , so we define \varepsilon^{*}_{R_{1}} as
\varepsilon^{*}_{R_{1}}=Q_{b}(1-\chi_{R_{1}})+\Pi h_{R_{1}}+\Pi\tilde{\Phi}(h_{R_{1}})=\Pi\tilde{P}_{u}\Pi^{-1}\left((1-\chi_{R_{1}})Q_{b}\right)+\Pi\tilde{\Phi}(h_{R_{1}}).
The boundedness of \tilde{P}_{u} (Lemma 7.1 (3)) and \tilde{\Phi} (7.23) implies that
\|\varepsilon^{*}_{R_{1}}\|_{H^{3}_{\langle x\rangle^{3}}}=o_{R_{1}\to\infty}(1),
(7.28)
and \varepsilon^{*}_{R_{1}}\in C^{\infty}_{c} from Lemma 7.1 (2). Hence the initial data
Q_{b;R_{1}}:=Q_{b}\chi_{R_{1}}+\varepsilon^{*}_{R_{1}}=Q_{b}+\Pi h_{R_{1}}+\Pi\tilde{\Phi}(h_{R_{1}})\in C^{\infty}_{c}
(7.29)
generates a blowup solution of (NLS) satisfying (7.20)-(7.22).
We also define
\tilde{\Phi}_{R_{1}}(h)=\tilde{\Phi}(h+h_{R_{1}})-\tilde{\Phi}(h_{R_{1}}),
(7.30)
which is also Lipschitz with constant \epsilon_{1}+o_{R_{1}}(1) from B^{\tilde{X}_{+}}_{\epsilon_{1}} to H^{1} due to (7.23).
(i) For R_{1}\gg 1 and \epsilon_{1}\ll 1 such that \|h_{R_{1}}\|_{\tilde{X}_{+}}+\epsilon_{1}<\epsilon_{0} , we define the manifold as
\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}
\displaystyle=
\displaystyle\left\{h\in B^{\tilde{X}_{+}}_{\epsilon_{1}}\cap\tilde{P}_{cs}\tilde{X}_{+}:Q_{b}+\Pi(h+h_{R_{1}})+\Pi\tilde{\Phi}(h+h_{R_{1}})\right\}
(7.31)
\displaystyle=
\displaystyle\left\{h\in B^{\tilde{X}_{+}}_{\epsilon_{1}}\cap\tilde{P}_{cs}\tilde{X}_{+}:Q_{b;R_{1}}+\Pi({\rm Id}+\tilde{\Phi}_{R_{1}})h\right\}
(7.32)
Therefore, (7.32) indicates {\mathcal{M}} being a Lipschitz finite-codimensional manifold in H^{1} , while (7.31) implies that it generates solutions satisfying (7.20)-(7.22). To conclude Theorem 1.5 (i), it remains to check the limit profile u^{*} (1.19) and compute the critical norm (1.20).
Recall that p_{c}=\frac{d(p-1)}{2} such that \dot{H}^{s_{c}}\hookrightarrow L^{p_{c}} . Denote \tilde{Q_{b}} and \tilde{\varepsilon} to be the rescaled functions
u=e^{i\tau(t)}\lambda(t)^{-\frac{2}{p-1}}(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)\left(t,\frac{\cdot}{\lambda(t)}\right)=:\tilde{Q}_{b}+\tilde{\varepsilon}.
Then (1.20) is a consequence of the following four estimates
\displaystyle\|\tilde{\varepsilon}(t)\chi_{R}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{c}}}
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle 1,
(7.33)
\displaystyle\|\tilde{Q}_{b}(t)\chi_{R}\|_{L^{p_{c}}}
\displaystyle\sim
\displaystyle|{\rm log}(T-t)|^{\frac{1}{p_{c}}},
(7.34)
\displaystyle\|\tilde{Q}_{b}(t)\chi_{R}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{c}}}
\displaystyle\sim
\displaystyle|{\rm log}(T-t)|^{\frac{1}{2}},
(7.35)
\displaystyle\|u(t)(1-\chi_{R})\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{c}}}
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle 1
(7.36)
where the constant is independent of t as t\to T . We will prove them and particularly show (1.19) during the proof of (7.36).
Proof of (7.33)-(7.35). For (7.33), we need the following estimate
\|f\chi_{M}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{c}}}\lesssim M^{\sigma-s_{c}}\|f\|_{\dot{H}^{\sigma}}.
(7.37)
for any M>0 , 0\leq s_{c}\leq\sigma<\min\{\frac{d}{2},1\} . It can be easily proven through high-low decomposition of f .
Applying this inequality and (1.10), we obtain
\displaystyle\|\tilde{\varepsilon}(t)\chi_{R}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{c}}}=\|\varepsilon(t)\chi_{R\lambda(t)^{-1}}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{c}}}\lesssim\|\varepsilon(t)\|_{\dot{H}^{\sigma}}(R\lambda(t)^{-1})^{\sigma-s_{c}}\lesssim 1
Next, recall from (1.7) that
|Q_{b}|\sim\langle x\rangle^{-\frac{2}{p-1}},\quad|Q_{b}^{\prime}|\lesssim\langle x\rangle^{-\frac{p+1}{p-1}}
(7.38)
and for a large enough R^{*}\gg 1 ,
|Q_{b}(x)-Q_{b}(x^{\prime})|\sim\langle x\rangle^{-\frac{2}{p-1}}\qquad\forall|x|\geq R^{*},\,\,|x^{\prime}|\geq 2|x|.
(7.39)
Hence the L^{p_{c}} estimate (7.34) follows immediately
\|\tilde{Q}_{b}\chi_{R}\|_{L^{p_{c}}}\sim\left(\int_{B_{2R\lambda(t)^{-1}}}\langle x\rangle^{-\frac{2}{p-1}p_{c}}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{p_{c}}}\sim|{\rm log}(T-t)|^{\frac{1}{p_{c}}}.
Using
\displaystyle\|\tilde{Q}_{b}(t)\chi_{R}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{c}}}^{2}=\|Q_{b}\chi_{\tilde{R}}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{c}}}^{2}
\displaystyle\sim
\displaystyle\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2d}}\frac{\left|(Q_{b}\chi_{\tilde{R}})(x-y)-(Q_{b}\chi_{\tilde{R}})(x)\right|^{2}}{|y|^{d+2s_{c}}}dxdy
\displaystyle=:
\displaystyle\iint F(x,y)dxdy
where \tilde{R}:=R\lambda(t)^{-1} .
The \dot{H}^{s_{c}} estimate comes from a careful partition of \mathbb{R}^{2d} . Indeed, let \tilde{R}=:2^{K} , with K\gg 1 , we define K^{*} the smallest integer greater than K . Define the disjoint partition \mathbb{R}^{2d}=\cup_{i=1}^{5}I_{i} with
\displaystyle I_{1}
\displaystyle=
\displaystyle\{|x|\geq 2^{K^{*}+1}\},
\displaystyle I_{2}
\displaystyle=
\displaystyle\{|x|<2^{K^{*}+1},|y|\geq 2^{K^{*}+1}\},
\displaystyle I_{3}
\displaystyle=
\displaystyle\{|x|<2^{K^{*}+1},|y|\in[2^{K^{*}-2},2^{K^{*}+1})\}
\displaystyle I_{4}
\displaystyle=
\displaystyle\{|x|<2^{K^{*}+1},|y|\in[2,2^{K^{*}-2})\}
\displaystyle I_{5}
\displaystyle=
\displaystyle\{|x|<2^{K^{*}+1},|y|<4\},
and further decompose I_{4} with k\in[2,K^{*}-3] by I_{4}=\cup_{k=2}^{K^{*}-3}\cup_{l=1}^{4}I_{4,k,l}
\displaystyle I_{4,k,1}
\displaystyle=
\displaystyle I_{4}\cap\{|x|<2^{k-2}\},
\displaystyle I_{4,k,2}
\displaystyle=
\displaystyle I_{4}\cap\{|x|\geq 2^{k-2},|x-y|<2^{k-2}\},
\displaystyle I_{4,k,3}
\displaystyle=
\displaystyle I_{4}\cap\{|x|\in[2^{k-2},2^{k+2}),|x-y|\geq 2^{k-2}\},
\displaystyle I_{4,k,4}
\displaystyle=
\displaystyle I_{4}\cap\{|x|\geq 2^{k+2}\}.
Brute force computations with the support of Q_{b}\chi_{\tilde{R}} and (7.38) yield
\displaystyle\iint_{I_{j}}F(x,y)dxdy\lesssim 1,
\displaystyle j=1,2,3,5;
\displaystyle\iint_{I_{4,k,l}}F(x,y)dxdy\lesssim 1,
\displaystyle l=1,2,3,4;\quad k\in[2,K^{*}-3].
And via (7.39), one can check the lower bound
\iint_{I_{4,k,l}}F(x,y)dxdy\sim 1,\qquad l=1,2;\quad k\in[{\rm log}R^{*}+5,K^{*}-3].
Thus by summing them up and using K^{*}\sim{\rm log}\tilde{R}\sim|{\rm log}(T-t)| , we obtain (7.35).
Proof of (7.36) and (1.19). As a preparation, we first consider the subcritical \dot{H}^{\tilde{\sigma}} convergence with 0\leq\tilde{\sigma}<s_{c} . Consider the Strichartz pair
r_{\tilde{\sigma}}=\frac{d(p+1)}{d+\tilde{\sigma}(p-1)},\quad\gamma_{\tilde{\sigma}}=\frac{4(p+1)}{(p-1)(d-2\tilde{\sigma})},\quad\frac{2}{\gamma_{\tilde{\sigma}}}+\frac{d}{r_{\tilde{\sigma}}}=\frac{d}{2}
And let \frac{1}{r_{\sigma}}-\frac{1}{r_{\tilde{\sigma}}}=\frac{\sigma-\tilde{\sigma}}{d}
such that \dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{\sigma}}\hookrightarrow\dot{W}^{\tilde{\sigma},r_{\tilde{\sigma}}} . Then by fractional Leibniz rule,
\|D^{\tilde{\sigma}}(u|u|^{p-1})\|_{L^{r_{\tilde{\sigma}}^{\prime}}}\lesssim\|D^{\tilde{\sigma}}u\|_{L^{r_{\tilde{\sigma}}}}^{p}\lesssim\|D^{\sigma}u\|_{L^{r_{\sigma}}}^{p}.
From Duhamel formula and Strichartz estimate of e^{it\Delta} , for 0\leq t_{1}\leq t_{2}<T
\displaystyle\|u(t_{1})-u(t_{2})\|_{\dot{H}^{\tilde{\sigma}}}\lesssim\|e^{it_{1}\Delta}u_{0}-e^{it_{2}\Delta}u_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{\tilde{\sigma}}}+\|D^{\sigma}u\|_{L^{p\gamma_{\tilde{\sigma}}^{\prime}}_{t}L^{r_{\sigma}}_{x}[t_{1},T)}^{p}
Note that
\|e^{it_{1}\Delta}u_{0}-e^{i(t_{2}-t_{0})\Delta}u_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{\tilde{\sigma}}}=\|(e^{i(t_{2}-t_{1})\Delta}-I)u(t_{0})\|_{\dot{H}^{\tilde{\sigma}}}=o_{|t_{2}-t_{1}|\to 0}(1)
and
\displaystyle\|D^{\sigma}u\|_{L^{p\gamma_{\tilde{\sigma}}^{\prime}}_{t}L^{r_{\sigma}}_{x}[t_{1},T)}^{p}\lesssim\left(\int_{t_{1}}^{T}\lambda(t)^{-2+\gamma_{\tilde{\sigma}}^{\prime}(s_{c}-\tilde{\sigma})}(\|Q_{b}\|_{\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{\sigma}}}^{p\gamma_{\tilde{\sigma}}^{\prime}}+\|\varepsilon(t)\|_{\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{\sigma}}}^{p\gamma_{\tilde{\sigma}}^{\prime}})dt\right)^{\frac{1}{\gamma_{\tilde{\sigma}}^{\prime}}}
\displaystyle\lesssim(T-t_{1})^{s_{c}-\tilde{\sigma}}(\|Q_{b}\|_{\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{\sigma}}}^{p}+\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{p\gamma_{\tilde{\sigma}}^{\prime}}_{\tau}\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{\sigma}}_{y}}^{p})=o_{t_{1}\to T}(1)
using Q_{b}\in\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{\sigma}} and the boundedness of \dot{H}^{\sigma} -Strichartz norm for \varepsilon with \frac{2}{p\gamma_{\tilde{\sigma}}^{\prime}}+\frac{d}{r_{\sigma}}>\frac{d}{2} an admissible pair. We know \{u(t)\} is a Cauchy sequence in \dot{H}^{\tilde{\sigma}} as t\to T , yielding the existence of limit profile u^{*}\in H^{\tilde{\sigma}} and the convergence (1.19). In particular, \|u(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}\dot{H}^{\tilde{\sigma}}[0,T]}<\infty .
By interpolation between \dot{H}^{\tilde{\sigma}} and \dot{H}^{\sigma} , (7.36) is reduced to bound \|u(t)(1-\chi_{R})\|_{\dot{H}^{\sigma}} . Decompose by
D^{\sigma}(u(t)(1-\chi_{R}))=(D^{\sigma}\tilde{Q}_{b})(1-\chi_{R})-[D^{\sigma},\chi_{R}]\tilde{Q}_{b}+D^{\sigma}(\tilde{\varepsilon}(1-\chi_{R})).
The last term involving \varepsilon is bounded by O(1) from (1.10). The O(1) bound of the first term follows a pointwise decay of D^{\sigma}Q_{b}
|D^{\sigma}Q_{b}(x)|=C_{\sigma,d}\left|\mathop{\rm lim}_{\epsilon\to 0}\int_{\{|y|\geq\epsilon\}}\frac{Q_{b}(x-y)-Q_{b}(x)}{|y|^{d+\sigma}}dy\right|\lesssim_{\sigma}\langle x\rangle^{-\frac{2}{p-1}-\sigma}
which is proven by splitting the integral in two and applying pointwise bounds of Q_{b} and \nabla Q_{b} (1.7) respectively. Finally, by the self-similar scaling and decay, we can uniformly bound
\mathop{\rm sup}_{t\in[0,T)}|\tilde{Q}_{b}(t,x)|\lesssim|x|^{-\frac{2}{p-1}}.
Thus the commutator has a pointwise bound
\displaystyle|[D^{\sigma},\chi_{R}]\tilde{Q}_{b}|
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle\left|\int\frac{\chi_{R}(x-y)-\chi_{R}(x)}{|y|^{d+\sigma}}\tilde{Q}_{b}(x-y)dy\right|
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle\mathbbm{1}_{B_{3R}}\left(|\cdot|^{-d-\sigma+1}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{\frac{R}{2}}}+|\cdot|^{-d-\sigma}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{\frac{R}{2}}^{c}}\right)*|\cdot|^{-\frac{2}{p-1}}
\displaystyle+\left(|\cdot|^{-d-\sigma}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{\frac{R}{2}}^{c}}\right)*(|\cdot|^{-\frac{2}{p-1}}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{2R}})
\displaystyle\lesssim_{R}
\displaystyle|x|^{-\frac{2}{p-1}}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{3R}}+\left(|\cdot|^{-d-\sigma}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{\frac{R}{2}}^{c}}\right)*(|\cdot|^{-\frac{2}{p-1}}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{2R}})
by again splitting the integral. This implies \|[D^{\sigma},\chi_{R}]\tilde{Q}_{b}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim 1 from Hölder and Young’s inequality.
This yields (7.36) and hence concludes (1.20).
(ii) Similar to the proof of Theorem 1.4, we reduce the proof to matching initial data in order to apply Theorem 7.3, and solve the finite dimensional equation through Brouwer argument.
For fixed \varepsilon_{0}\in B^{H^{1}}_{\epsilon_{1}} , we define the w_{0}^{\vec{\alpha}} from
(Q_{b;R_{1}}+\varepsilon_{0})(x)=\left(Q_{b;R_{1}}+w^{(\vec{\alpha})}_{0}\right)\left(\frac{x-x_{0}}{\lambda_{0}}\right)\lambda_{0}^{-\frac{d}{2}+s_{c}}e^{-i\theta_{0}},
and the statement in (ii) boils down to finding \vec{\alpha}\in B^{\mathbb{R}^{d+2}}_{C_{*}\|\varepsilon_{0}\|_{H^{1}}} such that
\tilde{\Phi}_{R_{1}}\left((1-\tilde{P}_{u})\Pi^{-1}w^{(\vec{\alpha})}_{0}\right)=\tilde{P}_{u}\Pi^{-1}w^{(\vec{\alpha})}_{0}
(7.40)
where \tilde{\Phi}_{R_{1}} is defined as (7.30).
To solve (7.40), we again take r_{\vec{\alpha};R_{1}}=Q_{b;R_{1}}(\lambda_{0}\cdot+x_{0})\lambda_{0}^{\frac{d}{2}-s_{c}}e^{i\theta_{0}}-Q_{b;R_{1}} and v_{\vec{\alpha}}=w_{0}^{\alpha}-r_{\vec{\alpha};R_{1}} .
From Assumption 1.2 and (6.34), we choose the basis of {\rm Ran}P_{u} as \{\varphi_{u;j}\}_{j=1}^{d+2}=\{\xi_{0},2\xi_{1},\zeta_{1},...,\xi_{d}\} , and define the modified eigenbasis \{\tilde{\varphi}_{u;j}\}_{j=1}^{d+2} for \tilde{P}_{u} correspondingly according to (7.9).
Notice that
\tilde{P}_{u}\Pi^{-1}\left(\nabla_{\vec{\alpha}}r_{\vec{\alpha};R_{1}}\right)\Big|_{\vec{\alpha}=\vec{0}}-\left(\xi_{0},2\xi_{2},\zeta_{1},...,\zeta_{d}\right)=o_{(\tilde{X}_{+})^{d+2}}(1),\quad{\rm as}\,\,R_{1}\to\infty,
due to the definition of Q_{b;R_{1}} (7.29) and vanishing of residual (7.28). Therefore, defining the parametrization using the basis \{\tilde{\varphi}_{u;j}\}_{j=1}^{d+2} , from the definition of \tilde{P}_{u} (7.10), we still have linear non-degeneracy that
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}\tilde{P}_{u}\Pi^{-1}\left(\vec{\alpha}\cdot(\nabla_{\alpha}r_{\alpha;R_{1}})\big|_{\vec{\alpha}=\vec{0}}\right)=({\rm Id}+o_{\mathbb{R}^{(d+2)\times(d+2)}}(1))\vec{\alpha},\quad{\rm as\,\,}R_{1}\to\infty.
The smallness of v_{\vec{\alpha}} , nonlinearity and Lipschitz constant of \tilde{\Phi}_{R_{1}} (7.30) enables us to conclude the Brouwer argument and hence the whole proof.
∎
Appendix A Fractional Non-stationary Phase
In Lemma 2.9 and Lemma 2.10, we want to make use of the H^{\delta} -regularity to integrate by parts and get decay from the quadratic oscillatory phase. For \delta<1 , we achieve this via mollification, with the mollifier’s size adapted to the decay r^{-1} we gain. The idea of this part is credited to Xiaodong Li.
Lemma A.1.
Let smooth cutoff function \psi\in C^{\infty}_{c,rad}(\mathbb{R}^{d}) , {\rm supp}~\psi\subset B_{2} , non-negative and \int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\psi(x)dx=1 . Define the rescaling of \psi (or general C^{\infty}_{c} functions) by \psi_{\lambda}(r):=\lambda^{-d}\psi(\lambda^{-1}r) . For f\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d}) and \delta\in(0,1) , we have
\displaystyle\left\|f*\psi_{|\cdot|^{-1}}\right\|_{L^{2}\{|x|\geq 1/2\}}
\displaystyle\lesssim_{\psi,d}
\displaystyle\|f\|_{L^{2}}
(A.1)
\displaystyle\left\|\partial_{r}(f*\psi_{|\cdot|^{-1}})|\cdot|^{-1+\delta}\right\|_{L^{2}\{|x|\geq 1/2\}}
\displaystyle\lesssim_{\psi,d,\delta}
\displaystyle\|f\|_{H^{\delta}}
(A.2)
\displaystyle\left\|\left(f-f*\psi_{|\cdot|^{-1}}\right)|\cdot|^{\delta}\right\|_{L^{2}\{|x|\geq 1/2\}}
\displaystyle\lesssim_{\psi,d,\delta}
\displaystyle\|f\|_{H^{\delta}}
(A.3)
where (A.1) also holds for general \psi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(B_{2}) with constant depending on \psi .
Proof.
(1) For (A.1), we have
\displaystyle\left\|f*\psi_{|\cdot|^{-1}}\right\|_{L^{2}\{|x|\geq 1/2\}}^{2}=\int_{B_{1/2}^{c}}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f(y)\psi_{|x|^{-1}}(x-y)dy\right|^{2}dx
\displaystyle\leq
\displaystyle\int_{B_{1/2}^{c}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|f(y)|^{2}|\psi_{|x|^{-1}}(x-y)|dy\right)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\psi_{|x|^{-1}}(x-y)|dy\right)dx
\displaystyle=
\displaystyle\|\psi\|_{L^{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|f(y)|^{2}\int_{B_{1/2}^{c}}|\psi(|x|(x-y))||x|^{d}dxdy
\displaystyle\leq
\displaystyle\|f\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\psi\|_{L^{1}}\|\psi\|_{L^{\infty}}\mathop{\rm sup}_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\left\{x:|x|\geq\frac{1}{2},|x-y|\leq 2|x|^{-1}\right\}}|x|^{d}dx.
We claim that
\mathop{\rm sup}_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\left\{x:|x|\geq\frac{1}{2},|x-y|\leq 2|x|^{-1}\right\}}|x|^{d}dx\lesssim_{d}1.
(A.4)
and (A.1) follows with general \psi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(B^{2}) . Indeed, when |y|\geq 8 , noticing that
\left\{x:|x|\geq\frac{1}{2},|x-y|\leq 2|x|^{-1}\right\}\subset\{x:|x-y|\leq 4\},
we have \frac{1}{2}|y|\leq|x|\leq 2|y| . So
\displaystyle\int_{\left\{x:|x|\geq\frac{1}{2},|x-y|\leq 2|x|^{-1}\right\}}|x|^{d}dx\leq\int_{|x-y|\leq 4|y|^{-1}}2^{d}|y|^{d}dx\lesssim_{d}1.
Also, when |y|\leq 8 ,
\displaystyle\int_{\left\{x:|x|\geq\frac{1}{2},|x-y|\leq 2|x|^{-1}\right\}}|x|^{d}dx\leq\int_{|x-y|\leq 4}|x|^{d}dx\leq\int_{B_{12}}|x|^{d}dx\lesssim_{d}1,
which verifies (A.4) and hence (A.1).
(2) For (A.2), we first compute
\displaystyle\partial_{r}(f*\psi_{|x|^{-1}})(x)
\displaystyle=
\displaystyle(f*\partial_{\lambda}\psi_{|x|^{-1}})(x)(r^{-1})^{\prime}+\frac{x}{|x|}\cdot(f*\nabla(\psi_{|x|^{-1}}))(x)
\displaystyle=
\displaystyle|x|^{-1}\left(f*[(d+(\cdot)\cdot\nabla)\psi]_{|x|^{-1}}\right)(x)+x\cdot(f*(\nabla\psi)_{|x|^{-1}})(x)
Let \tilde{\psi}:=(d+x\cdot\nabla)\psi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(B^{2}) , we have
\displaystyle\left\|\partial_{r}(f*\psi_{|\cdot|^{-1}})|\cdot|^{-1+\delta}\right\|_{L^{2}\{|x|\geq 1/2\}}
\displaystyle\leq
\displaystyle\left\|(f*\tilde{\psi}_{|\cdot|^{-1}})|\cdot|^{-2+\delta}\right\|_{L^{2}\{|x|\geq 1/2\}}+\left\|(f*(\nabla\psi)_{|x|^{-1}})|\cdot|^{\delta}\right\|_{L^{2}\{|x|\geq 1/2\}}.
Using (A.1), the first term is bounded by C_{\psi,d,\delta}\|f\|_{L^{2}} . So it suffices to prove
\displaystyle\left\|\left(f*(\nabla\psi)_{|\cdot|^{-1}}\right)|\cdot|^{\delta}\right\|_{L^{2}\{|x|\geq 1/2\}}
\displaystyle\lesssim_{\psi,d,\delta}
\displaystyle\|f\|_{\dot{H}^{\delta}}
(A.5)
For (A.5),
\displaystyle\left\|\left(f*(\nabla\psi)_{|\cdot|^{-1}}\right)|\cdot|^{\delta}\right\|_{L^{2}\{|x|\geq 1/2\}}^{2}=\int_{B_{1/2}^{c}}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f(x-y)(\nabla\psi)_{|x|^{-1}}(y)dy\right|^{2}|x|^{2\delta}dx
\displaystyle=
\displaystyle\int_{B_{1/2}^{c}}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(f(x-y)-f(x)\right)(\nabla\psi)_{|x|^{-1}}(y)dy\right|^{2}|x|^{2\delta}dx
\displaystyle\leq
\displaystyle\int_{B_{1/2}^{c}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left|f(x-y)-f(x)\right|\left(\frac{2|x|^{-1}}{|y|}\right)^{\frac{d+2\delta}{2}}|(\nabla\psi)_{|x|^{-1}}(y)|dy\right)^{2}|x|^{2\delta}dx
\displaystyle\leq
\displaystyle\int_{B_{1/2}^{c}}2^{d+2\delta}|x|^{-(d+2\delta)}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\left|f(x-y)-f(x)\right|^{2}}{|y|^{d+2\delta}}dy\right)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|(\nabla\psi)_{|x|^{-1}}(y)|^{2}dy\right)|x|^{2\delta}dx
\displaystyle\leq
\displaystyle\|\nabla\psi\|_{L^{2}}^{2}2^{d+2\delta}\int_{B^{c}_{1/2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\left|f(x-y)-f(x)\right|^{2}}{|y|^{d+2\delta}}dydx
where we used the vanishing \int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\nabla\psi)_{\lambda}(x)dx=\vec{0} for any \lambda>0 and its support and scaling.
With the equivalent expression of \dot{H}^{\delta} norm (2.36), the above computation implies (A.5) and hence (A.2).
(3) Finally, for (A.3), note that \int\psi(x)dx=1 indicates
\displaystyle\left\|\left(f-f*\psi_{|\cdot|^{-1}}\right)|\cdot|^{\delta}\right\|_{L^{2}\{|x|\geq 1/2\}}^{2}
\displaystyle=
\displaystyle\int_{B_{1/2}^{c}}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(f(x-y)-f(x)\right)\psi_{|x|^{-1}}(y)dy\right|^{2}|x|^{2\delta}dx
\displaystyle\leq
\displaystyle\|\psi\|_{L^{2}}^{2}2^{d+2\delta}\int_{B^{c}_{1/2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\left|f(x-y)-f(x)\right|^{2}}{|y|^{d+2\delta}}dydx.
The last inequality follows almost the same computation as the above one for (A.5).
∎
Appendix B Commutator estimates of multiplication in \dot{H}^{\sigma}
We consider several boundedness and commutator estimates for scalar potential V in \dot{H}^{\sigma} . Recall our polynomial decay assumption on potential
|V(x)|\lesssim\langle x\rangle^{-\alpha},\quad|\nabla V(x)|\lesssim\langle x\rangle^{-\alpha-1}
(1.27)
for some \alpha>0 .
Lemma B.1.
For d\geq 1 , 0<\sigma<\min\left\{1,\frac{d}{2}\right\} and V\in C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}) , for 2\leq p<\frac{d}{\sigma} ,
\|D^{\sigma}VD^{-\sigma}\|_{L^{2}\to L^{p^{\prime}}}+\|D^{\sigma}VD^{-\sigma}\|_{L^{p}\to L^{2}}\lesssim_{d,p,\sigma}\|V\|_{\dot{W}^{\sigma,\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\sigma}{d}-\frac{1}{p}\right)^{-1}}}
(B.1)
In particular, for V satisfying (1.27) with \alpha>0 , D^{\sigma}VD^{-\sigma}\in\mathcal{L}(L^{2}\to L^{p^{\prime}})\cap\mathcal{L}(L^{p}\to L^{2}) for \frac{1}{2}\geq\frac{1}{p}>\max\{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{d},\frac{\sigma}{d}\} .
Proof.
It is an easy application of Sobolev embedding and the fractional Lebnitz rule (see for example [27, Theorem 1])
\|D^{s}(f_{1}f_{2})\|_{L^{r}}\lesssim_{d,s,r,p_{1},p_{2},q_{1},q_{2}}\|D^{s}f_{1}\|_{L^{p_{1}}}\|f_{2}\|_{L^{q_{1}}}+\|f_{1}\|_{L^{p_{2}}}\|D^{s}f_{2}\|_{L^{q_{2}}}
(B.2)
with 1>s>0 , r\in(1,\infty) , p_{1},p_{2},q_{1},q_{2}\in(1,\infty] satisfying \frac{1}{r}=\frac{1}{p_{1}}+\frac{1}{q_{1}}=\frac{1}{p_{2}}+\frac{1}{q_{2}} and f,g\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d}) .
∎
Moreover, the shape of V indicates a stronger bound: \|\langle x\rangle^{\alpha}Vf\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2}} in L^{2} . This remains almost true after conjugating V with fractional derivatives.
Lemma B.2.
Let d\geq 1 , 0<\sigma<\min\left\{1,\frac{d}{2}\right\} and V\in C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}) satisfying (1.27) with parameter \alpha>0 . We have the following statements:
(i)
For 0\leq\beta<\min\left\{\alpha,1\right\} and any f\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d}) , we have
\|\langle x\rangle^{\beta}D^{\sigma}VD^{-\sigma}f\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2}}.
(B.3)
(ii)
For 0\leq\beta<\min\{\alpha,\frac{d}{2}-\sigma\}
and f\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d}) , we have
\|\langle x\rangle^{\beta}D^{-\sigma}VD^{\sigma}f\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2}}
(B.4)
(iii)
Let 0<\alpha+\beta<\gamma<1 , we have
\|\langle x\rangle^{\alpha}D^{\sigma}\langle\cdot\rangle^{\beta}D^{-\sigma}\langle\cdot\rangle^{-\gamma}f\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2}}.
(B.5)
Consequently, \langle x\rangle^{\beta}\in\mathcal{L}(\dot{H}^{\sigma}_{\langle x\rangle^{\gamma}}\to\dot{H}^{\sigma}_{\langle x\rangle^{\alpha}}) .
(iv)
Suppose additionally V\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}) and |\nabla^{2}V(x)|\lesssim\langle x\rangle^{-\alpha-2} , then for r<\frac{d}{\sigma} , \frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{r}\in(1-\frac{\sigma}{d},1) , we have
\|D^{\sigma}[V,D^{1-\sigma}]D^{-\sigma}f\|_{L^{r^{\prime}}}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{q}}.
(B.6)
Consequently, [V,D^{1-\sigma}]\in{\mathcal{L}}(\dot{W}^{\sigma,q}\to\dot{W}^{\sigma,r^{\prime}}) .
Proof.
(i) Since \langle x\rangle^{\beta}V satisfies the polynomial decay condition (1.27) with parameter \alpha-\beta>0 , Lemma B.1 suggests that it suffices to estimate the commutator
\left\|[D^{\sigma},\langle\cdot\rangle^{\beta}]VD^{-\sigma}f\right\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2}}.
(B.7)
Without less of generality, we assume \alpha\in(\beta,1) .
With the physical representation of D^{\sigma} and the cancellation from the commutator, we have the decomposition
\displaystyle\left|[D^{\sigma},\langle\cdot\rangle^{\beta}]VD^{-\sigma}f\right|(x)=C\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\langle x\rangle^{\beta}-\langle x-y\rangle^{\beta}}{|y|^{d+\sigma}}(VD^{-\sigma}f)(x-y)dy\right|
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle\int_{|y|\geq 2\langle x\rangle}|y|^{-(d+\sigma+\alpha-\beta)}\left(|\cdot|^{-(d-\sigma)}*|f|\right)(x-y)dy
(B.8)
\displaystyle+
\displaystyle\int_{|y|\leq 2\langle x\rangle}|y|^{-(d+\sigma-1)}\langle x\rangle^{\beta-1}\langle x-y\rangle^{-\alpha}\left[\left(|\cdot|^{-(d-\sigma)}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{4\langle x\rangle}}\right)*|f|\right](x-y)dy
(B.9)
\displaystyle+
\displaystyle\int_{|y|\leq 2\langle x\rangle}|y|^{-(d+\sigma-1)}\langle x\rangle^{\beta-1}\langle x-y\rangle^{-\alpha}\left[\left(|\cdot|^{-(d-\sigma)}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{4\langle x\rangle}^{c}}\right)*|f|\right](x-y)dy
(B.10)
where |V| is substituted by \langle x\rangle^{-\alpha} and the fractional weight is bounded by
\left|\langle x\rangle^{\beta}-\langle x-y\rangle^{\beta}\right|\langle x-y\rangle^{-\alpha}\lesssim\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}|y|^{\beta-\alpha}&|y|\geq 2\langle x\rangle,\\
|y|\langle x\rangle^{\beta-1}\langle x-y\rangle^{-\alpha}&|y|\leq 2\langle x\rangle.\end{array}\right.
Now we will bound the L^{2} -norm of (B.8)-(B.10) with \|f\|_{L^{2}} .
For (B.8), using Hölder and Hardy-Littlewood-Sobolev inequality, we get
\displaystyle\eqref{eqcom01}\lesssim\||\cdot|^{-(d+\sigma+\alpha-\beta)}\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d+2\sigma}}(B_{2\langle x\rangle}^{c})}\||\cdot|^{-(d-\sigma)}*f\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2\sigma}}}\lesssim\langle x\rangle^{-\frac{d}{2}-\alpha+\beta}\|f\|_{L^{2}}.
Since \alpha>\beta , we get \|\eqref{eqcom01}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2}} .
For (B.9), noticing that |z|\geq 4\langle x\rangle and |y|\leq 2\langle x\rangle imply |x-y-z|\sim|z| , so
\displaystyle\eqref{eqcom02}\lesssim\langle x\rangle^{\beta-1}\int_{B_{2\langle x\rangle}}|y|^{-(d+\sigma-1)}\langle x-y\rangle^{-\alpha}dy\int_{B_{4\langle x\rangle}^{c}}\frac{|f(z)|}{|z|^{d-\sigma}}dz\lesssim\langle x\rangle^{-\frac{d}{2}-\alpha+\beta}\|f\|_{L^{2}}
where the first integral is bounded by \langle x\rangle^{1-\sigma-\alpha} when \alpha<d via elementary computation. Therefore this term has the desired bound.
Finally, we write (B.10) as
\displaystyle\eqref{eqcom03}=\langle x\rangle^{\beta-1}\left(|\cdot|^{-(d+\sigma-1)}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{2\langle x\rangle}}\right)*\left[\langle\cdot\rangle^{-\alpha}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{3\langle x\rangle}}\left(|\cdot|^{-(d-\sigma)}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{4\langle x\rangle}}*|f|\right)\right](x).
Then we can estimate their L^{2} norm on dyadic annulus with k\geq 0
\displaystyle\|\eqref{eqcom03}\|_{L^{2}(B_{2^{k+1}}-B_{2^{k}})}
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle 2^{-k(1-\beta)}\||\cdot|^{-(d+\sigma-1)}\|_{L^{p_{1}}(B_{2^{k+3}})}\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{-\alpha}\|_{L^{p_{2}}(B_{2^{k+3}})}
\displaystyle\cdot\||\cdot|^{-(d-\sigma)}\|_{L^{p_{3}}(B_{2^{k+3}})}\|f\|_{L^{2}}
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle 2^{-k(\alpha-\beta)}\|f\|_{L^{2}}.
where we choose p_{1},p_{2},p_{3}\in(1,\infty) such that \frac{1}{p_{1}}+\frac{1}{p_{2}}+\frac{1}{p_{3}}=2 , p_{1}<\frac{d}{d+\sigma-1} , p_{2}<\frac{d}{\alpha} and p_{3}<\frac{d}{d-\sigma} which is possible when \alpha<1 . The contribution on B_{1} can be bounded similarly, and we conclude the estimate of (B.10) by summing all these estimates up using \alpha>\beta .
