Combining these three parts, we obtain (B.7) and hence (B.3).
(ii) Without loss of generality, we assume \alpha<\frac{d}{2}-\sigma . Because \langle x\rangle^{\beta}V\in L^{\infty} , it suffices to show
\left\|\langle x\rangle^{\beta}D^{-\sigma}[D^{\sigma},V]f\right\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2}}.
(B.11)
Notice that
\displaystyle\langle x\rangle^{\beta}D^{-\sigma}[D^{\sigma},V]f(x)
\displaystyle=
\displaystyle C\langle x\rangle^{\beta}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{1}{|y|^{d-\sigma}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{V(x-y)-V(x-y-z)}{|z|^{d+\sigma}}f(x-y-z)dzdy.
Once again, we decompose the integral region and estimate the weights correspondingly. Here the estimate for interaction is
|V(x_{1})-V(x_{2})|\lesssim\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\langle x_{1}\rangle^{-\alpha-1}|x_{1}-x_{2}|&|x_{1}-x_{2}|\leq\frac{1}{2}\langle x_{1}\rangle\\
\langle x_{2}\rangle^{-\alpha}&|x_{1}-x_{2}|\in[\frac{1}{8}\langle x_{1}\rangle,8\langle x_{1}\rangle]\\
\langle x_{1}\rangle^{-\alpha}&|x_{1}-x_{2}|\geq 2\langle x_{1}\rangle.\end{array}\right.
(B.12)
So we will partition y and z into 3\times 3 regions:
(1)
\left\{|y|\leq\frac{1}{2}\langle x\rangle,|z|\leq\frac{1}{4}\langle x\rangle\right\},\left\{|y|\leq\frac{1}{2}\langle x\rangle,|z|\in\left[\frac{1}{4},4\right]\langle x\rangle\right\},\left\{|y|\leq\frac{1}{2}\langle x\rangle,|z|\geq 4\langle x\rangle\right\};
(2)
\left\{|y|\in\left[\frac{1}{2},2\right]\langle x\rangle,|z|\leq\frac{1}{4}\langle x-y\rangle\right\},\left\{|y|\in\left[\frac{1}{2},2\right]\langle x\rangle,|z|\in\left[\frac{1}{4},4\right]\langle x-y\rangle\right\},\\
\left\{|y|\in\left[\frac{1}{2},2\right]\langle x\rangle,|z|\geq 4\langle x-y\rangle\right\};
(3)
\left\{|y|\geq 2\langle x\rangle,|z|\leq\frac{1}{4}\langle y\rangle\right\},\left\{|y|\geq 2\langle x\rangle,|z|\in\left[\frac{1}{4},4\right]\langle y\rangle\right\},\left\{|y|\geq 2\langle x\rangle,|z|\geq 4\langle y\rangle\right\} .
Therefore we have
\displaystyle\left|\langle x\rangle^{\beta}D^{-\sigma}[D^{\sigma},V]f(x)\right|
(B.13)
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle\langle x\rangle^{-1-\alpha+\beta}\left(|\cdot|^{-(d-\sigma)}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{\frac{1}{2}\langle x\rangle}}\right)*\left(|\cdot|^{-(d+\sigma-1)}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{\frac{1}{4}\langle x\rangle}}\right)*|f|(x)
\displaystyle+
\displaystyle\langle x\rangle^{-d-\sigma+\beta}\int_{B_{\frac{1}{2}\langle x\rangle}}|y|^{-(d-\sigma)}\int_{B_{4\langle x\rangle}}\frac{|f(x-y-z)|}{\langle x-y-z\rangle^{\alpha}}dzdy
(B.14)
\displaystyle+
\displaystyle\langle x\rangle^{-\alpha+\beta}\left(|\cdot|^{-(d-\sigma)}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{\frac{1}{2}\langle x\rangle}}\right)*\left(|\cdot|^{-(d+\sigma)}\mathbbm{1}_{B^{c}_{4\langle x\rangle}}\right)*|f|(x)
(B.15)
\displaystyle+
\displaystyle\langle x\rangle^{-d+\sigma+\beta}\int_{B_{2\langle x\rangle}-B_{\frac{1}{2}\langle x\rangle}}\langle x-y\rangle^{-\alpha-1}\int_{B_{\frac{1}{4}\langle x-y\rangle}}\frac{|f(x-y-z)|}{|z|^{d+\sigma-1}}dzdy
(B.16)
\displaystyle+
\displaystyle\langle x\rangle^{-d+\sigma+\beta}\int_{B_{2\langle x\rangle}-B_{\frac{1}{2}\langle x\rangle}}\langle x-y\rangle^{-d-\sigma}\int_{B_{4\langle x-y\rangle}-B_{\frac{1}{4}\langle x-y\rangle}}\frac{|f(x-y-z)|}{\langle x-y-z\rangle^{\alpha}}dzdy
(B.17)
\displaystyle+
\displaystyle\langle x\rangle^{-d+\sigma+\beta}\int_{B_{2\langle x\rangle}-B_{\frac{1}{2}\langle x\rangle}}\langle x-y\rangle^{-\alpha}\int_{B^{c}_{4\langle x-y\rangle}}\frac{|f(x-y-z)|}{|z|^{d+\sigma}}dzdy
(B.18)
\displaystyle+
\displaystyle\langle x\rangle^{\beta}\int_{B_{2\langle x\rangle}^{c}}|y|^{-(d-\sigma+\alpha+1)}\int_{B_{\frac{1}{4}\langle y\rangle}}\frac{|f(x-y-z)|}{|z|^{d+\sigma-1}}dzdy
(B.19)
\displaystyle+
\displaystyle\langle x\rangle^{\beta}\int_{B_{2\langle x\rangle}^{c}}|y|^{-2d}\int_{B_{4\langle y\rangle}-B_{\frac{1}{4}\langle y\rangle}}\frac{|f(x-y-z)|}{\langle x-y-z\rangle^{\alpha}}dzdy
(B.20)
\displaystyle+
\displaystyle\langle x\rangle^{\beta}\int_{B_{2\langle x\rangle}^{c}}|y|^{-(d-\sigma+\alpha)}\int_{B_{4\langle y\rangle}^{c}}\frac{|f(x-y-z)|}{|z|^{d+\sigma}}dzdy.
(B.21)
We partition these 9 terms into four groups to estimate.
(1)
For (B.13) and (B.15), we exploit the convolution structure to estimate the L^{2} norm on non-homogeneous dyadic annulus, resulting in \|\eqref{eqcom41}\|_{L^{2}(B_{2^{k+1}}-B_{2^{k}})}\lesssim 2^{-k(\alpha-\beta)}\|f\|_{L^{2}} , \|\eqref{eqcom41}\|_{L^{2}(B_{1})}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2}} , and similar for (B.15).
(2)
For (B.14), (B.17) and (B.20), a straightforward application of Hölder’s inequality implies pointwise bound by \langle x\rangle^{-\frac{d}{2}-\alpha+\beta}\|f\|_{L^{2}} .
(3)
We address (B.19) and (B.21) similarly to (B.13) and (B.15), but with an additional dyadic decomposition for the domain of y to fix the integration domain of z . For example,
\displaystyle\|\eqref{eqcom47}\|_{L^{2}(B_{2^{k+1}}-B_{2^{k}})}
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle 2^{k\beta}\sum_{j\geq k}\left\|\left(|\cdot|^{-(d-\sigma+\alpha+1)}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{2^{j+1}}-B_{2^{j}}}\right)*\left(|\cdot|^{-(d+\sigma-1)}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{2^{j}}}\right)*|f|\right\|_{L^{2}}
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle 2^{k\beta}\sum_{j\geq k}2^{-k\alpha}\|f\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{-k(\alpha-\beta)}\|f\|_{L^{2}}.
(4)
For (B.16)-(B.18), we first apply a change of variable y^{\prime}:=x-y\in B_{4\langle x\rangle} so that \langle x\rangle only affects the size of the integration domain of y^{\prime} .
By considering x and y dyadically, we can finally arrive at the same pointwise bound \langle x\rangle^{-\frac{d}{2}+\beta-\alpha}\|f\|_{L^{2}} . For instance, when x\in B_{2^{k+1}}-B_{2^{k}} ,
\displaystyle\eqref{eqcom46}\lesssim\langle x\rangle^{-d+\sigma+\beta}\int_{B_{2^{k+3}}}\langle y^{\prime}\rangle^{-\alpha}\int_{B_{4\langle y^{\prime}\rangle}^{c}}\frac{|f(y^{\prime}-z)|}{|z|^{d+\sigma}}dzdy
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle 2^{(-d+\sigma+\beta)k}\sum_{0\leq j\leq k}\int_{B_{2^{j+3}}-B_{2^{j+2}}}2^{-j\alpha}\left[\left(|\cdot|^{-(d+\sigma)}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{2^{j+1}}^{c}}\right)*|f|\right](y^{\prime})dy^{\prime}
\displaystyle+2^{(-d+\sigma+\beta)k}\int_{B_{4}}\left[\left(|\cdot|^{-(d+\sigma)}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{8}^{c}}\right)*|f|\right](y^{\prime})dy^{\prime}
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle 2^{(-d+\sigma+\beta)k}\sum_{0\leq j\leq k}2^{j(\frac{d}{2}-\sigma-\alpha)}\|f\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{\left(-\frac{d}{2}+\beta-\alpha\right)k}\|f\|_{L^{2}}.
These bounds conclude the proof.
(iii) It suffices to estimate the commutator
\|\langle x\rangle^{\alpha}[D^{\sigma},\langle\cdot\rangle^{\beta}]D^{-\sigma}\langle\cdot\rangle^{-\gamma}f\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2}}.
(B.22)
We decompose the domain of integration for y into
\{|y|\geq 2\langle x\rangle\} and \{|y|\leq 2\langle x\rangle\} , and further decompose the domain of z by comparing |z| with \frac{1}{4}|y| or 4\langle x\rangle respectively. Estimating |\langle x\rangle^{\beta}-\langle x-y\rangle^{\beta}| , |x-y-z| and \langle z\rangle correspondingly, we obtain
\displaystyle\left|\langle x\rangle^{\alpha}[D^{\sigma},\langle\cdot\rangle^{\beta}]D^{-\sigma}\langle\cdot\rangle^{-\gamma}f(x)\right|
\displaystyle=
\displaystyle C\left|\langle x\rangle^{\alpha}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\langle x\rangle^{\beta}-\langle x-y\rangle^{\beta}}{|y|^{d+\sigma}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\langle z\rangle^{-\gamma}f(z)}{|x-y-z|^{d-\sigma}}dzdy\right|
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle\langle x\rangle^{\alpha}\int_{B_{2\langle x\rangle}^{c}}|y|^{-(d+\sigma-\beta)}\int_{B_{\frac{1}{4}|y|}}|y|^{-(d-\sigma)}\langle z\rangle^{-\gamma}|f(z)|dzdy
(B.23)
\displaystyle+
\displaystyle\langle x\rangle^{\alpha}\int_{B_{2\langle x\rangle}^{c}}|y|^{-(d+\sigma-\beta+\gamma)}\left(|\cdot|^{-(d-\sigma)}*|f|\right)(x-y)dy
(B.24)
\displaystyle+
\displaystyle\langle x\rangle^{\alpha+\beta-1}\left(|\cdot|^{-(d+\sigma-1)}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{2\langle x\rangle}}\right)*\left(|\cdot|^{-(d-\sigma)}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{8\langle x\rangle}}\right)*\left(\langle\cdot\rangle^{-\gamma}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{4\langle x\rangle}}|f|\right)(x)
(B.25)
\displaystyle+
\displaystyle\langle x\rangle^{\alpha+\beta-1}\int_{B_{2\langle x\rangle}}|y|^{-(d+\sigma-1)}dy\int_{B_{4\langle x\rangle}^{c}}\frac{|f(z)|}{|z|^{d-\sigma+\gamma}}dz
(B.26)
For (B.23), (B.24) and (B.26), Hölder’s and Hardy-Littlewood-Sobolev inequalities imply a pointwise bound by \langle x\rangle^{-(d/2+\gamma-\alpha-\beta)}\|f\|_{L^{2}} , so their L^{2} -norm is bounded by \|f\|_{L^{2}} . For (B.25), similar to (B.10), we can control their L^{2} -norm on dyadic annulus B_{2^{k+1}}-B_{2^{k}} by 2^{-k(\gamma-\alpha-\beta)}\|f\|_{L^{2}} and on B_{1} by \|f\|_{L^{2}} . Combining these bounds, we get (B.22) and hence (B.5).
(iv) We decompose
D^{\sigma}[V,D^{1-\sigma}]D^{-\sigma}f=[V,D^{1-\sigma}]f+\left[D^{\sigma},[V,D^{1-\sigma}]\right]D^{-\sigma}f
and estimate both terms. The estimates are similar to the previous cases, with extra simplicity in that we can always set \alpha=0 .
Term 1: [V,D^{1-\sigma}]f . By partitioning |y|\geq 2\langle x\rangle or |y|\leq 2\langle x\rangle , we have
\displaystyle\left|[V,D^{1-\sigma}]f\right|
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle\langle x\rangle^{-1}\left(|\cdot|^{-(d-\sigma)}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{2\langle x\rangle}})*|f|\right)+\left(|\cdot|^{-(d-\sigma+1)}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{2\langle x\rangle}}^{c})*|f|\right)
\displaystyle+
\displaystyle\langle x\rangle^{-(d+1-\sigma)}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{B_{4\langle x\rangle}},|f|\right)_{L^{2}}
For the first two terms, we decompose \langle x\rangle in dyadic regions and apply Young’s inequality to bound the L^{r^{\prime}} norm, and the last term can be estimated directly through Hölder’s inequality. The range of \frac{1}{r^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{q}\in(0,\frac{\sigma}{d}) ensures the integrability and summability.
Term 2: \left[D^{\sigma},[V,D^{1-\sigma}]\right]D^{-\sigma}f . Denote F:=D^{-\sigma}f and \frac{1}{q_{\sigma}}:=\frac{1}{q}+\frac{\sigma}{d} . Then from Sobolev embedding, it suffices to show
\|\left[D^{\sigma},[V,D^{1-\sigma}]\right]F\|_{L^{r^{\prime}}}\lesssim\|F\|_{L^{q_{\sigma}}}.
By expanding both fractional derivative and exploit cancellations, we obtain
\left[D^{\sigma},[V,D^{1-\sigma}]\right]F=C\iint\frac{V(x)-V(x-y)-V(x-z)+V(x-y-z)}{|y|^{d+1-\sigma}|z|^{d+\sigma}}F(x-y-z)dydz.
By partitioning |y| and |z| both according to \frac{1}{4}\langle x\rangle and use the pointwise bound of \nabla V , \nabla^{2}V , we obtain
\displaystyle\left|\left[D^{\sigma},[V,D^{1-\sigma}]\right]F(x)\right|
(B.27)
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle\langle x\rangle^{-2}\cdot\left[\left(|\cdot|^{-(d-\sigma)}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{\frac{1}{4}\langle x\rangle}}\right)*\left(|\cdot|^{-(d-1+\sigma)}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{\frac{1}{4}\langle x\rangle}}\right)*|F|\right]
\displaystyle+
\displaystyle\int_{B_{\frac{1}{4}\langle x\rangle}^{c}}\int_{B_{\frac{1}{4}\langle x\rangle}}\frac{\langle x\rangle^{-1}+\langle x-y\rangle^{-1}}{|y|^{d+1-\sigma}|z|^{d-1+\sigma}}|F(x-y-z)|dydz
(B.28)
\displaystyle+
\displaystyle\int_{B_{\frac{1}{4}\langle x\rangle}}\int_{B_{\frac{1}{4}\langle x\rangle}^{c}}\frac{\langle x\rangle^{-1}+\langle x-z\rangle^{-1}}{|y|^{d-\sigma}|z|^{d+\sigma}}|F(x-y-z)|dydz
(B.29)
\displaystyle+
\displaystyle\left(|\cdot|^{-(d+1-\sigma)}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{\frac{1}{4}\langle x\rangle}^{c}}\right)*\left(|\cdot|^{-(d+\sigma)}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{\frac{1}{4}\langle x\rangle}^{c}}\right)*|F|
(B.30)
Noticing that s:=\frac{1}{q_{\sigma}}-\frac{1}{r^{\prime}}\in(0,\frac{\sigma}{d}) , for (B.27) and (B.30), we can partition the range of \langle x\rangle dyadically and apply Young’s inequality with suitable L^{1+} norm on the homogeneous parts so as to exploit \|F\|_{L^{q_{\sigma}}} , which generates additional growth \langle x\rangle^{\frac{\sigma}{d}} in (B.27) absorbed by \langle x\rangle^{-2} , and additional decay \langle x\rangle^{-\frac{\sigma}{d}} in (B.30) for summability. The terms (B.28) and (B.29) are symmetric, and the part with \langle x\rangle^{-1} can be estimated using exactly the same strategy as (B.27) or (B.30). Lastly, we estimate the part in (B.28) with \langle x-y\rangle^{-1} dyadically as
\displaystyle\left\|\int_{B_{\frac{1}{4}\langle x\rangle}^{c}}\int_{B_{\frac{1}{4}\langle x\rangle}}\frac{\langle x-y\rangle^{-1}}{|y|^{d+1-\sigma}|z|^{d-1+\sigma}}|F(x-y-z)|dydz\right\|_{L^{r^{\prime}}(B_{2^{n+1}}-B_{2^{n}})}
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle\left\|(|\cdot|^{-(d-1+\sigma)}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{2^{n+2}}})*\left[\langle\cdot\rangle^{-1}\cdot\left((|\cdot|^{-(d+1-\sigma)}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{2^{n-3}}^{c}})*|F|\right)\right]\right\|_{L^{r^{\prime}}(B_{2^{n+1}})}
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle\left\||\cdot|^{-(d-1+\sigma)}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{2^{n+2}}}\right\|_{L^{1}}\cdot\left\||\cdot|^{-(d+1-\sigma)}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{2^{n-3}}^{c}}\right\|_{L^{(1-s)^{-1}}}\cdot\|F\|_{L^{(\frac{1}{r^{\prime}}+s)^{-1}}}
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle 2^{-sn}\|F\|_{L^{q_{\sigma}}}.
Summing over n\geq 0 using s>0 implies the desired control.
∎
Appendix C Nonlinear estimates in fractional Sobolev space
The following estimates in fractional Sobolev space are for the nonlinearity in (6.1). We first state the main result and give some remarks.
Lemma C.1.
Given d\geq 1 and W\in C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{C}-\{0\}) satisfying
|\nabla W(x)|\leq C_{W}\langle x\rangle^{-a}|W(x)|,\quad\forall\,x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
(C.1)
for some C_{W}>0 , a\in[0,1] , then for p>1 , s\in(0,1) , r\in(1,\infty) and \varepsilon\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{C}) , we have the following estimates for
R(\varepsilon):=\bigg[|W+\varepsilon|^{p-1}(W+\varepsilon)-|W|^{p-1}W-\frac{p+1}{2}|W|^{p-1}\varepsilon-\frac{p-1}{2}|W|^{p-3}W^{2}{\overline{\varepsilon}}\bigg]
(C.2)
with \tilde{r},r_{1},r_{2}\in(1,\infty) such that
\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{\tilde{r}}\in\left[0,\frac{as}{d}\right) , \frac{1}{r}=\frac{1}{r_{1}}+\frac{1}{r_{2}} and (p-1)r_{1}>1 :
•
If 1<p\leq 2 ,
\|R(\varepsilon)\|_{\dot{W}^{s,r}}\lesssim_{d,p,s,r,\tilde{r},r_{1},r_{2},a,C_{W}}\||\varepsilon|^{p}\|_{L^{\tilde{r}}}+\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{(p-1)r_{1}}}^{p-1}\|\varepsilon\|_{\dot{W}^{s,r_{2}}}
(C.3)
•
If p>2 ,
\begin{split}\|R(\varepsilon)\|_{\dot{W}^{s,r}}\lesssim_{d,p,s,r,\tilde{r},r_{1},r_{2},a,C_{W}}&\||\varepsilon|^{p}\|_{L^{\tilde{r}}}+\||W|^{p-2}|\varepsilon|^{2}\|_{L^{\tilde{r}}}\\
+&\left(\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{(p-1)r_{1}}}^{p-1}+\||W|^{p-2}|\varepsilon|\|_{L^{r_{1}}}\right)\|\varepsilon\|_{\dot{W}^{s,r_{2}}}.\end{split}
(C.4)
Remark C.2.
When p is large, these estimates easily follow the fractional chain rule for F^{\prime\prime}(W+t\varepsilon) with F(z):=|z|^{p-1}z . However, when p-1\ll 1 (as d\gg 1 ), F^{\prime\prime}(u) may not even be continuous for generic u\in C^{\infty}_{c} , let alone estimates on D^{s} derivatives.
Fortunately, we exploit the specialty of F and W here: the nonlinearity F only has a singularity at 0 while W does not vanish anywhere. Thus, with an appropriate estimate for \nabla W , the Taylor expansion of F around W will be good enough.
Proof.
Denote W=:\Sigma+i\Theta , \varepsilon=:\varepsilon_{1}+i\varepsilon_{2} and R_{1}(\varepsilon):=\Re R(\varepsilon) , R_{2}(\varepsilon):=\Im R(\varepsilon) .
Also let F_{i}:\mathbb{R}^{2}\to\mathbb{R} with i=1,2 to be
F_{1}(a,b)=(a^{2}+b^{2})^{\frac{p-1}{2}}a,\quad F_{2}(a,b)=(a^{2}+b^{2})^{\frac{p-1}{2}}b.
(C.5)
Then for i=1,2 ,
\displaystyle R_{i}(\varepsilon)=F_{i}\left(\Sigma+\varepsilon_{1},\Theta+\varepsilon_{2}\right)-F_{i}(\Sigma,\Theta)-\partial_{1}F_{i}(\Sigma,\Theta)\varepsilon_{1}-\partial_{2}F_{i}(\Sigma,\Theta)\varepsilon_{2}
From the expression of F_{i} , it is easy to see F_{i}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2}-\{0\},\mathbb{R}) and
\left|\nabla^{n}F_{i}(a,b)\right|\lesssim_{n}(a^{2}+b^{2})^{\frac{p-n}{2}},\quad\forall\,n\geq 0,\,\forall\,(a,b)\neq(0,0).
(C.6)
So if (W+t\varepsilon)(x)\neq 0 for any t\in[0,1] , the following representation also holds
R_{i}(\varepsilon)(x)=\sum_{j,k=1}^{2}\varepsilon_{j}(x)\varepsilon_{k}(x)\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{j}\partial_{k}F_{i}(\Sigma(x)+t\varepsilon_{1}(x),\Theta(x)+t\varepsilon_{2}(x))(1-t)dt
(C.7)
We will prove (C.3)-(C.4) in two steps, following the strategy of [11] or [65]. For simplicity, we only prove the case 1<p\leq 2 while the p>2 case follows in a similar way.
Step 1. Pointwise estimates.
We first derive pointwise estimate for difference of R(\varepsilon)(x+h)-R(\varepsilon)(x) with x,h\in\mathbb{R}^{d} . For notational simplicity, we define the difference operator
\displaystyle\Delta_{h}f(x):=f(x+h)-f(x).
Then we claim the following estimates:
\begin{split}|\Delta_{h}R(\varepsilon)(x)|\lesssim_{p,C_{W},a}&\left(|\varepsilon(x+h)|^{p}+|\varepsilon(x)|^{p}\right)\min\{1,\langle x\rangle^{-a}|h|\}\\
+&\left(|\varepsilon(x+h)|^{p-1}+|\varepsilon(x)|^{p-1}\right)\left|\Delta_{h}\varepsilon(x)\right|;\end{split}
(C.8)
We only consider estimates for R_{1}(\varepsilon) as an example.
From (C.1), there exists a c_{0}=c_{0}(C_{W},a)>0 such that
|W(x)-W(x+h)|\leq\frac{1}{3}|W(x)|,\quad\forall\,x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\,\forall\,|h|\leq\langle x\rangle^{a}c_{0}.
(C.9)
Then we distinguish two cases by comparing |h| with \langle x\rangle^{a}c_{0} .
Case 1. |h|\geq\langle x\rangle^{a}c_{0} . It suffices to bound R_{1}(\varepsilon)(x) and R_{1}(\varepsilon)(x+h) separately by |R(\varepsilon)(\cdot)|\lesssim_{p}|\varepsilon(\cdot)|^{p} .
If |\varepsilon(x)|\geq\frac{1}{2}|W(x)| , the trivial bound |R_{1}(\varepsilon)(x)|\lesssim_{p}|W(x)|^{p}+|\varepsilon(x)|^{p} suffices. If |\varepsilon(x)|\leq\frac{1}{2}|W(x)| , from (C.6)
we know
\mathop{\rm sup}_{t\in[0,1]}\left|\nabla^{2}F_{1}(\Sigma(x)+t\varepsilon_{1}(x),\Theta(x)+t\varepsilon_{2}(x))\right|\lesssim_{p}|W(x)|^{p-2}.
Thus (C.7) yields
|R_{1}(\varepsilon)(x)|\lesssim|W(x)|^{p-2}|\varepsilon(x)|^{2}\lesssim_{p}|\varepsilon(x)|^{p}.
Case 2. |h|\leq\langle x\rangle^{a}c_{0} . We consider the following three subcases.
Case 2.1. |\varepsilon(x)|\geq\frac{1}{6}|W(x)| . Since |\varepsilon(x)|\gtrsim|W(x)|\sim|W(x+h)| , we can estimate most interaction terms in a direct fashion
\displaystyle|\Delta_{h}(|W|^{p-1}\Sigma)|+|\Delta_{h}(|W|^{p-1}\varepsilon_{1})|+|\Delta_{h}(|W|^{p-3}(\Sigma^{2}-\Theta^{2})\varepsilon_{1})|
\displaystyle\qquad+|\Delta_{h}(|W|^{p-3}\Sigma\Theta\varepsilon_{2})|
\displaystyle\lesssim_{p,C_{W}}
\displaystyle|h|\langle x\rangle^{-a}|W(x)|^{p-1}\left(|\varepsilon(x)|+|\varepsilon(x+h)|+|W(x)|\right)+|W(x)|^{p-1}|\Delta_{h}\varepsilon(x)|
\displaystyle\lesssim_{p}
\displaystyle|h|\langle x\rangle^{-a}\left(|\varepsilon(x)|^{p}+|\varepsilon(x+h)|^{p}\right)+|\varepsilon(x)|^{p-1}|\Delta_{h}\varepsilon(x)|
where we used |\nabla W(x+th)|\leq C_{W}\langle x+th\rangle^{-a}|W(x+th)|\sim\langle x\rangle^{-a}|W(x)| for t\in[0,1] to bound the difference of W . For the first term, we exploit the elementary estimate
\left|\frac{A^{\alpha}-B^{\alpha}}{A-B}\right|\sim_{\alpha}\max\{A,B\}^{\alpha-1},\quad\alpha>0
(C.10)
for A,B>0 . Thus
\displaystyle\left|\Delta_{h}\left(|W+\varepsilon|^{p-1}(\Sigma+\varepsilon_{1})\right)\right|
\displaystyle\leq
\displaystyle\left|\frac{\Delta_{h}|W+\varepsilon|^{p-1}}{\Delta_{h}|W+\varepsilon|}\left(\Delta_{h}|W+\varepsilon|\right)(\Sigma+\varepsilon_{1})(x+h)\right|+|W+\varepsilon|^{p-1}(x)\left|\Delta_{h}(\Sigma+\varepsilon_{1})\right|
\displaystyle\lesssim_{p,C_{W}}
\displaystyle\left(|W+\varepsilon|^{p-1}(x+h)+|W+\varepsilon|^{p-1}(x)\right)\left(|h|\langle x\rangle^{-a}|W(x)|+|\Delta_{h}\varepsilon|\right)
\displaystyle\lesssim_{p}
\displaystyle|h|\langle x\rangle^{-a}\left(|\varepsilon(x)|^{p}+|\varepsilon(x+h)|^{p}\right)+\left(|\varepsilon(x)|^{p-1}+|\varepsilon(x+h)|^{p-1}\right)|\Delta_{h}\varepsilon(x)|.
These estimates implies both (C.8).
Case 2.2. |\varepsilon(x)|\leq\frac{1}{6}|W(x)|\leq|\varepsilon(x+h)| . Noticing the symmetry between x and x+h term and that |\varepsilon(x+h)|\gtrsim|W(x)|\sim|W(x+h)| , the bound in this case follows exactly the same argument as Case 2.1 above.
Case 2.3. |\varepsilon(x)|\leq\frac{1}{6}|W(x)| , |\varepsilon(x+h)|\leq\frac{1}{6}|W(x)| .
In the following computation, we slightly abuse notation to denote W:=W(x) , W^{\prime}:=W(x+h) and similarly for \varepsilon and \varepsilon^{\prime} . Applying (C.7), we get
\displaystyle R_{1}(\varepsilon)(x+h)-R_{1}(\varepsilon)(x)
\displaystyle=
\displaystyle\sum_{j,k=1}^{2}\Bigg[\varepsilon_{j}^{\prime}\varepsilon_{k}^{\prime}\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{j}\partial_{k}F_{1}(W^{\prime}+t\varepsilon^{\prime})(1-t)dt-\varepsilon_{j}\varepsilon_{k}\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{j}\partial_{k}F_{1}(W+t\varepsilon)(1-t)dt\Bigg]
\displaystyle=
\displaystyle\sum_{j,k=1}^{2}\left(\Delta_{h}(\varepsilon_{j}\varepsilon_{k})\right)\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{j}\partial_{k}F_{1}(W^{\prime}+t\varepsilon^{\prime})(1-t)dt
\displaystyle+
\displaystyle\sum_{j,k=1}^{2}\varepsilon_{j}\varepsilon_{k}\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)\int_{0}^{1}[(W^{\prime}-W)+t(\varepsilon^{\prime}-\varepsilon)]\cdot\nabla\partial_{j}\partial_{k}F_{1}\big((1-s)(W+t\varepsilon)+s(W^{\prime}+t\varepsilon^{\prime})\big)dsdt.
Thanks to (C.9) and the smallness of \varepsilon(x) , \varepsilon(x+h) , we have
\left|(1-s)(W+t\varepsilon)+s(W^{\prime}+t\varepsilon^{\prime})-W\right|\leq\frac{1}{2}|W(x)|,\quad\forall\,t,s\in[0,1],
and hence all the derivatives acting on F_{1} are well-defined. This and (C.6) for k=3 imply the boundedness
\displaystyle|\Delta_{h}R_{1}(\varepsilon)(x)|\lesssim_{p,C_{W}}|\Delta_{h}\varepsilon|(|\varepsilon^{\prime}|+|\varepsilon|)|W|^{p-2}+|\varepsilon|^{2}\left(|h|\langle x\rangle^{-a}|W|+|\Delta_{h}\varepsilon|\right)|W|^{p-3}.
which leads to the bounds (C.8).
Step 2. Littlewood-Paley and maximal function estimates. Next, we apply the argument in [65] to derive estimates in fractional Sobolev spaces. Denote the homogeneous Littlewood-Paley decomposition as
P_{k}f=\mathscr{F}^{-1}(\psi_{k}\cdot)\hat{f})=\check{\psi}_{k}*f,\quad k\in\mathbb{Z},
and the Hardy-Littlewood maximal operator as M . Since \psi_{k}=\psi(2^{-k}\cdot) with \psi\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{c}(B_{2}-B_{\frac{1}{2}}) for k\in\mathbb{Z} , we have \check{\psi}_{k}=2^{kd}\check{\psi}(2^{k}\cdot)\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d}) and
\displaystyle|\check{\psi}_{k}(x)|
\displaystyle\lesssim_{d,m}
\displaystyle 2^{dk}\langle 2^{k}x\rangle^{-m},\quad\forall\,m\geq 0,
(C.11)
\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\psi_{k}(x)dx
\displaystyle=
\displaystyle 0.
(C.12)
The Littlewood-Paley theory indicates
\left\|f\right\|_{\dot{W}^{s,r}}\sim_{d,s,r}\left\|\sum_{j=-\infty}^{+\infty}2^{js}P_{j}f\right\|_{L^{r}}\sim_{d,s,r}\left\|\left(\sum_{j}2^{2js}|P_{j}f(\cdot)|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{r}}
(C.13)
for s\in(0,1) and r\in(1,\infty) . We recall the following estimates involving maximal functions from
[65, Chap. 2 Lemma 3.1-3.5]
\displaystyle\begin{split}\int|P_{k}u(x)-P_{k}u(y)|\cdot|\check{\psi}_{j}(x-y)|dy\lesssim_{d}\min\{2^{k-j},1\}M(P_{k}u)(x),\end{split}
(C.14)
\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int|P_{k}u(x)-P_{k}u(y)|\cdot|\check{\psi}_{j}(x-y)|H(y)dy\\
&\qquad\lesssim_{d}\min\{2^{k-j},1\}\left[M(P_{k}u)(x)MH(x)+M(|P_{k}u|H)(x)\right]\end{split}
(C.15)
where H is a positive function and k,j\in\mathbb{Z} .
Now we derive a pointwise estimate for the function in (C.13) and finish the proof.
Using the vanishing condition (C.12) and the pointwise estimates (C.8), we have
\displaystyle\sum_{j}2^{2js}|P_{j}R(\varepsilon)(x)|^{2}\leq\sum_{j}2^{2js}\left[\int|R(\varepsilon)(y)-R(\varepsilon)(x)||\check{\psi}_{j}(x-y)|dy\right]^{2}
(C.16)
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle\sum_{j}2^{2js}\left[\int\left(|\varepsilon(y)|^{p}+|\varepsilon(x)|^{p}\right)\min\{1,\langle x\rangle^{-a}|x-y|\}|\check{\psi}_{j}(x-y)|dy\right]^{2}
\displaystyle+
\displaystyle\sum_{j}2^{2js}\left[\int\left(|\varepsilon(y)|^{p-1}+|\varepsilon(x)|^{p-1}\right)|\varepsilon(x)-\varepsilon(y)||\check{\psi}_{j}(x-y)|dy\right]^{2}
(C.17)
For (C.16), we claim
\eqref{eqdifterm1}\lesssim(M(|\varepsilon|^{p})(x))^{2}\langle x\rangle^{-2as}.
(C.18)
Indeed, the decay (C.11) implies
\int|\check{\psi}_{j}(x-y)|\min\{1,\langle x\rangle^{-a}|x-y|\}dy\lesssim\min\{2^{-j}\langle x\rangle^{-a},1\}.
Then the bound on |\varepsilon(x)|^{p} term follows
\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{2js}\min\{2^{-2j}\langle x\rangle^{-2a},1\}\sim\langle x\rangle^{-2as}
thanks to s\in(0,1) . Likewise, for the other term involving |\varepsilon(y)|^{p} , it suffices to show the maximal function bound
A_{j}(x):=\int|\varepsilon(y)|^{p}|\check{\psi}_{j}(x-y)|\min\{1,\langle x\rangle^{-a}|x-y|\}dy\lesssim\min\{2^{-j}\langle x\rangle^{-a},1\}M(|\varepsilon|^{p})(x).
Let j_{0}\in\mathbb{Z} such that 2^{j_{0}}\leq\langle x\rangle^{-a}<2^{j_{0}+1} . Take m=d+2 , then for j\leq j_{0} ,
\displaystyle A_{j}(x)
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle\int_{|z|\leq 2^{-j}}|\varepsilon(x-z)|^{p}2^{dj}dz+\sum_{k\geq-j}\int_{2^{k}\leq|z|\leq 2^{k+1}}|\varepsilon(x-z)|^{p}2^{dj}2^{-(j+k)m}dz
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle M(|\varepsilon|^{p})(x)\left(1+\sum_{k\geq-j}2^{(d-m)(j+k)}\right)\lesssim M(|\varepsilon|^{p})(x);
and for j>j_{0} ,
\displaystyle A_{j}(x)
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle\int_{|z|\leq 2^{-j}}|\varepsilon(x-z)|^{p}2^{dj}2^{j_{0}-j}dz+\sum_{k=-j}^{-j_{0}}\int_{2^{k}\leq|z|\leq 2^{k+1}}|\varepsilon(x-z)|^{p}2^{dj}2^{j_{0}+k}2^{-m(j+k)}dz
\displaystyle+
\displaystyle\sum_{k\geq-j_{0}+1}\int_{2^{k}\leq|z|\leq 2^{k+1}}|\varepsilon(x-z)|^{p}2^{dj}2^{-m(j+k)}dz
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle 2^{j_{0}-j}M(|\varepsilon|^{p})(x).
For (C.17), we apply Littlewood-Paley decomposition to \varepsilon(x)-\varepsilon(y) and then use (C.14)-(C.15) to get
(C.17)
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle\sum_{j}2^{2js}\left|\sum_{k}\int\left(|\varepsilon(y)|^{p-1}+|\varepsilon(x)|^{p-1}\right)|P_{k}\varepsilon(x)-P_{k}\varepsilon(y)||\check{\psi}_{j}(x-y)|dy\right|^{2}
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle\sum_{j}2^{2js}\left[\sum_{k}\min\{2^{k-j},1\}M(P_{k}\varepsilon)(x)M(|\varepsilon|^{p-1})(x)\right]^{2}
\displaystyle+\sum_{j}2^{2js}\left[\sum_{k}\min\{2^{k-j},1\}M(|P_{k}\varepsilon|\cdot|\varepsilon|^{p-1})(x)\right]^{2}
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle\left[M(|\varepsilon|^{p-1})(x)\right]^{2}\sum_{k}2^{2ks}\left[M(P_{k}\varepsilon)(x)\right]^{2}+\sum_{k}2^{2ks}\left[M(|P_{k}\varepsilon|\cdot|\varepsilon|^{p-1})(x)\right]^{2}
where the last inequality follows Young’s inequality.
From (C.13) and these estimates of (C.11)-(C.12), we bound the \dot{W}^{s,r} norm by
\displaystyle\|R(\varepsilon)\|_{\dot{W}^{s,r}}
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle\left\|M(|\varepsilon|^{p})\langle x\rangle^{-as}\right\|_{L^{r}}+\left\|M(|\varepsilon|^{p-1})(x)\left\|2^{ks}M(P_{k}\varepsilon)(x)\right\|_{l^{2}_{k}}\right\|_{L^{r}}
\displaystyle+\left\|\left\|2^{ks}M(|P_{k}\varepsilon|\cdot|\varepsilon|^{p-1})(x)\right\|_{l^{2}_{k}}\right\|_{L^{r}}
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle\||\varepsilon|^{p}\|_{L^{\tilde{r}}}+\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{(p-1)r_{1}}}^{p-1}\left\|\left\|2^{ks}|P_{k}\varepsilon(x)|\right\|_{l^{2}_{k}}\right\|_{L^{r_{2}}}+\left\||\varepsilon|^{p-1}(x)\left\|2^{ks}|P_{k}\varepsilon|(x)\right\|_{l^{2}_{k}}\right\|_{L^{r}}
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle\||\varepsilon|^{p}\|_{L^{\tilde{r}}}+\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{(p-1)r_{1}}}^{p-1}\|\varepsilon\|_{\dot{W}^{s,r_{2}}}
with \frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{\tilde{r}}\in\left[0,\frac{as}{d}\right) , \frac{1}{r}=\frac{1}{r_{1}}+\frac{1}{r_{2}} and (p-1)r_{1}>1 . In the second-to-last inequality, we invoked the Fefferman-Stein maximal theorem [21]
\left\|\|M(h_{k})(x)\|_{l^{2}_{k}}\right\|_{L^{q}_{x}}\lesssim\left\|\|h_{k}(x)\|_{l^{2}_{k}}\right\|_{L^{q}_{x}}
for a sequence of functions \{h_{k}\} and q\in(1,\infty) .
∎
Next, we present a difference estimate in L^{p} space.
Lemma C.3.
Given d\geq 1 and W\in C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{C}) , p>1 and u,v\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{C}) , for R(u) defined as in (C.2),
we have difference estimate for r,r_{1},r_{2}\in[1,\infty] with \frac{1}{r}=\frac{1}{r_{1}}+\frac{p-1}{r_{2}} :
•
If 1<p\leq 2 ,
\|R(u)-R(v)\|_{L^{r}}\lesssim_{d,p,r,r_{1},r_{2}}\|u-v\|_{L^{r_{1}}}\left(\|u\|_{L^{r_{2}}}^{p-1}+\|v\|_{L^{r_{2}}}^{p-1}\right);
(C.19)
•
If p>2 ,
\begin{split}&\|R(u)-R(v)\|_{L^{r}}\\
\lesssim_{d,p,s,r,r_{1},r_{2}}&\|u-v\|_{L^{r_{1}}}\left(\|u\|_{L^{r_{2}}}^{p-1}+\|v\|_{L^{r_{2}}}^{p-1}+\left(\|u\|_{L^{r_{2}}}+\|v\|_{L^{r_{2}}}\right)\|W\|_{L^{r_{2}}}^{p-2}\right).\end{split}
(C.20)
Proof.
It suffices to prove the corresponding pointwise estimate for any x
\displaystyle|R(u)-R(v)|\lesssim_{p}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}|u-v|(|u|^{p-1}+|v|^{p-1})&1<p\leq 2,\\
|u-v|(|u|^{p-1}+|v|^{p-1}+(|u|+|v|)|W|^{p-2})&p\geq 2.\end{array}\right.
The proof is similar to Step 1 in Lemma C.1 and thus is omited.
∎
Finally, we give a pointwise derivative estimate for nonlinearity.
Lemma C.4.
Let d\geq 1 and W\in C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{C}-\{0\}) satisfying (C.1) with a\in[0,1] , p>1 and u,v\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{C}) , for R(u) defined as in (C.2). Then we have
|\nabla R(\varepsilon)|\lesssim\left|\begin{array}[]{ll}|\varepsilon|^{p-1}\cdot(|\nabla\varepsilon|+|\varepsilon|\langle x\rangle^{-a})&1<p\leq 2\\
(|\varepsilon|^{p-2}+|W|^{p-2})\cdot|\varepsilon|\cdot(|\nabla\varepsilon|+|\varepsilon|\langle x\rangle^{-a})&p\geq 2\end{array}\right.
(C.22)
Proof.
It is straightforward computation of Lebnitz rule after decomposing into two cases |\varepsilon(x)|\leq\frac{1}{2}|W(x)| or |\varepsilon(x)|\geq\frac{1}{2}|W(x)| .
∎
Appendix D Local Well-posedness in \dot{H}^{\sigma}
In this section, we prove local well-posedness of the intercritical Schrödinger equation (NLS)
\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}i\partial_{t}u+\Delta u+u|u|^{p-1}=0\\
u_{|t=0}=u_{0}\in\dot{H}^{\sigma}\end{array}\right.
and the renormalized system (1.21) (we denote the time variable as t )
\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}i\partial_{t}Z+\mathcal{H}Z=F,\\
Z_{|t=0}=Z_{0}\in\dot{H}^{\sigma}\times\dot{H}^{\sigma}.\end{array}\right.
with {\mathcal{H}} , F=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}-R(\varepsilon,Q_{b})\\
\overline{R(\varepsilon,Q_{b})}\end{array}\right) defined by (1.4), (1.26) and (6.1).
Here we assume s_{c}<\sigma\leq\alpha_{c}=\frac{d}{d+2-2s_{c}} above the critical scaling. We will also use the exponents r_{1},r_{2},q_{1},\beta as in (6.21)-(6.22). We begin with the original system (NLS).
Proposition D.1.
Suppose d\geq 1 and 0<s_{c}<\min\left\{1,\frac{d}{2}\right\} and consider the Cauchy problem (NLS). For any s_{c}<\sigma\leq\alpha_{c} , we have the following local well-posedness result: when d\neq 2 , for any initial data u_{0}\in\dot{H}^{\sigma} , there exists T=T(\|u\|_{\dot{H^{\sigma}}}) such that (NLS) has a unique solution
u\in C^{0}_{t}\dot{H}^{\sigma}_{x}\cap L^{2}_{t}\dot{W}_{x}^{\sigma,2^{*}}([-T,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d})=:Y_{d};
when d=2 , for any u_{0}\in\dot{H}^{\sigma} and r\in[2,\infty) , there exists T=T(\|u\|_{\dot{H^{\sigma}}},r) such that
(NLS) has a unique solution
u\in C^{0}_{t}\dot{H}^{\sigma}_{x}\cap L^{r^{\prime}}_{t}\dot{W}_{x}^{\sigma,r}([-T,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{2})=:Y_{2}.
Moreover, \|u\|_{Y_{d}}\lesssim\|u_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{\sigma}} and the solution map u_{0}\mapsto u is Lipschitz.
Proof.
The proof follows standard Banach fixed point argument and the \dot{H}^{\sigma} -subcritical nature of (NLS). For clarity, we only prove the case d\neq 2 . The d=2 case is no more than a substitution by non-endpoint Strichartz norm.
Define
\Psi_{u_{0}}(u):=e^{it\Delta}u_{0}+i\int_{0}^{t}e^{i(t-s)\Delta}(|u|^{p-1}u)(s)ds.
We will show \Psi_{u_{0}} is contractive in some suitable metric space.
Let M:=\|u_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{\sigma}} . Define the closed ball
X_{T,M}:=\left\{u\in C^{0}_{t}\dot{H}^{\sigma}_{x}\cap L^{2}_{t}\dot{W}_{x}^{\sigma,2^{*}}([-T,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}):\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}\dot{H}^{\sigma}_{x}\cap L^{2}_{t}\dot{W}_{x}^{\sigma,2^{*}}}\leq 2C_{0}M\right\}
(D.3)
and metric
\mathfrak{d}_{T}(u,v):=\|u-v\|_{L^{q_{0}}_{t}L^{r_{0}}_{x}([-T,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}
(D.4)
where C_{0} is the constant in the Strichartz estimate for e^{it\Delta} .
Here r_{0}\in(2,2^{*}) satisfies
\frac{d}{2}-\frac{d}{r_{0}}=\frac{\frac{d}{2}-\sigma}{\frac{d}{2}-s_{c}}
(D.5)
and q_{0}=2\frac{\frac{d}{2}-s_{c}}{\frac{d}{2}-\sigma} such that (q_{0},r_{0}) is Strichartz admissible.
From s_{c}<\sigma\leq\alpha_{c}=\frac{d}{d+2-2s_{c}} , we know \sigma\leq\mathrm{RHS\,\,of\,\,}\eqref{eqrsigma}<\min\left\{1,\frac{d}{2}\right\} and thus (\dot{H}^{\sigma}\cap\dot{W}^{\sigma,2^{*}})(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow L^{r_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}) . Consequently, (X_{T,M},\mathfrak{d}_{T}) is a complete metric space for T<\infty .
We first check \Psi_{u_{0}}:X_{T,M}\to X_{T,M} .
By Strichartz estimate,
\displaystyle\|\Psi_{u_{0}}(u)\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}\dot{H}^{\sigma}_{x}\cap L^{2}_{t}\dot{W}_{x}^{\sigma,2^{*}}}\leq C_{0}\|u_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{\sigma}}+C_{1}\left\|D^{\sigma}(|u|^{p-1}u)\right\|_{L_{t}^{q_{1}^{\prime}}L^{r_{1}^{\prime}}_{x}}.
Then by fractional chain rule for C^{1} nonlinearity (see [11]), we get
\displaystyle\left\|D^{\sigma}(|u|^{p-1}u)\right\|_{L_{t}^{q_{1}^{\prime}}L^{r_{1}^{\prime}}_{x}}\lesssim\left\|\|u\|_{\dot{W}_{x}^{\sigma,r_{1}}}\|u\|_{L^{pr_{2}}_{x}}^{p-1}\right\|_{L^{q_{1}^{\prime}}_{t}}\lesssim\|u\|_{L^{q_{1}}_{t}\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}}_{x}}^{p}T^{\beta}\lesssim M^{p}T^{\beta}
Thus the onto property of \Psi_{u_{0}} is guaranteed by taking T\sim\|u_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{\sigma}}^{-\frac{p-1}{\beta}}=M^{-\frac{p-1}{\beta}} .
Next, we check that \Psi_{u_{0}} is a contraction concerning the metric \mathfrak{d}_{T} . Taking \tilde{r}_{0} as \frac{1}{\tilde{r}_{0}}=\frac{1}{r_{0}^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{r_{0}} , we then have \dot{H}^{\sigma}\hookrightarrow L^{\tilde{r}_{0}(p-1)} . Thus
\displaystyle\|\Psi_{u_{0}}(u)-\Psi_{u_{0}}(v)\|_{L^{q_{0}}_{t}L^{r_{0}}_{x}}\lesssim\||u|^{p-1}u-|v|^{p-1}v\|_{L^{q_{0}^{\prime}}_{t}L^{r_{0}^{\prime}}_{x}}
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle\left\|\|u-v\|_{L_{x}^{r_{0}}}\left(\|u\|_{L_{x}^{\tilde{r}_{0}(p-1)}}^{p-1}+\|v\|_{L_{x}^{\tilde{r}_{0}(p-1)}}^{p-1}\right)\right\|_{L^{q_{0}^{\prime}}_{t}}
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle\|u-v\|_{L^{q_{0}}_{t}L^{r_{0}}_{x}}\left(\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}\dot{H}^{\sigma}_{x}}^{p-1}+\|v\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}\dot{H}^{\sigma}_{x}}^{p-1}\right)T^{\beta}\lesssim\|u-v\|_{L^{q_{0}}_{t}L^{r_{0}}_{x}}\|u_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{\sigma}}^{p-1}T^{\beta}.
Here the same \beta comes from \frac{1}{q_{0}^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{q_{0}}=\frac{\sigma-s_{c}}{\frac{d}{2}-s_{c}}=\beta . By further shrinking T by a constant if necessary, we can conclude that \Psi_{u_{0}} is a contraction on (X_{T,M},\mathfrak{d}_{T}) for d\neq 2 . We omit the standard arguments for the rest statements in this proposition.
∎
Next, we derive a similar local-wellposedness result of the renormalized system (1.21).
Proposition D.2.
Suppose d\geq 1 and 0<s_{c}<\min\left\{1,\frac{d}{2}\right\} and consider the Cauchy problem (1.21). For any s_{c}<\sigma\leq\alpha_{c} , we have the following local well-posedness result: when d\neq 2 , for any initial data Z_{0}\in(\dot{H}^{\sigma})^{2} , there exists T=T(\|Z_{0}\|_{(\dot{H^{\sigma}})^{2}}) such that (1.21) has a unique solution
Z\in C^{0}_{t}([-T,T],(\dot{H}^{\sigma}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))^{2})\cap L^{2}_{t}([-T,T],(\dot{W}_{x}^{\sigma,2^{*}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))^{2})=:\tilde{Y}_{d};
when d=2 , for any Z_{0}\in(\dot{H}^{\sigma})^{2} and r\in[2,\infty) , there exists T=T(\|Z_{0}\|_{(\dot{H^{\sigma}})^{2}},r) such that
(NLS) has a unique solution
Z\in C^{0}_{t}([-T,T],(\dot{H}^{\sigma}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))^{2})\cap L^{r^{\prime}}_{t}([-T,T],(\dot{W}_{x}^{\sigma,r}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))^{2})=:\tilde{Y}_{2}.
Moreover, \|Z\|_{\tilde{Y}_{d}}\lesssim\|Z_{0}\|_{(\dot{H}^{\sigma})^{2}} and the solution map Z_{0}\mapsto Z is Lipschitz.
Proof.
This is still a contraction argument as above with estimates similar to the proof of Proposition D.1 and Theorem 6.4. Again, for simplicity, we only discuss d\neq 2 and 1<p\leq 2 case.
We view the potential as a source term and write
\displaystyle\Psi_{Z_{0}}(Z):=e^{i\tau({\mathcal{H}}_{b}-ibs_{c})}Z_{0}+i\int_{0}^{\tau}e^{i(\tau-\tau^{\prime})({\mathcal{H}}_{b}-ibs_{c})}(F-VZ)(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}.
As in Proposition D.1, we take a distance given by the weaker norm L^{q_{0}}_{\tau}L^{r_{0}}_{y} .
Since we only consider well-posedness local in time, we may assume T_{0}\leq 1 . Thereafter, all exponential timewise weight can be neglected and e^{it({\mathcal{H}}_{b}-ibs_{c})} has the Strichartz estimates in both \dot{H}^{\sigma} and L^{2} .
Using the nonlinear estimate from Lemma C.1 and linear estimate Lemma B.1 for potential, we get the estimate similar to (6.31) (with exponents borrowed there)
\displaystyle\|F\|_{\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}^{\prime}}}
\displaystyle\lesssim
\displaystyle\|Z\|_{\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}}\cap\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}^{-}}}^{p},\quad\|VZ\|_{\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}^{\prime}}}\lesssim\|Z\|_{\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}}}.
Like (6.2), the space-time estimate follows
\displaystyle\|F-VZ\|_{L^{q_{1}^{\prime}}_{\tau}\dot{W}^{\sigma,r_{1}^{\prime}}_{y}}\lesssim(\|Z\|_{\tilde{Y}_{d}}^{p}+\|Z\|_{\tilde{Y}_{d}})T^{\beta}
Now the Strichartz estimate for e^{it({\mathcal{H}}_{b}-ibs_{c})} in \dot{H}^{\sigma} yields the onto estimate straightforwardly.
As for the contraction estimate, notice that
\displaystyle|R(Z_{1},Q_{b})-R(Z_{1}^{\prime},Q_{b})|\lesssim|Z_{1}-Z_{1}^{\prime}|(|Z_{1}|^{p-1}+|Z_{1}^{\prime}|^{p-1}+|Q_{b}|^{p-1}).
Then mimicking Proposition D.1, we get
\displaystyle\|(F-VZ)-(F^{\prime}-VZ^{\prime})\|_{L^{q_{0}^{\prime}}_{\tau}L^{r_{0}^{\prime}}_{y}}\lesssim\|Z-Z^{\prime}\|_{L^{q_{0}}_{\tau}L^{r_{0}}_{y}}\left(\|Z\|_{L^{\infty}_{\tau}\dot{H}^{\sigma}_{y}}^{p-1}+\|Z^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\tau}\dot{H}^{\sigma}_{y}}^{p-1}+\|Q_{b}\|_{\dot{H}^{\sigma}}^{p-1}\right)T^{\beta}
which suffices to conclude the contraction estimate.
∎
